# Phase 4.10 Function Library Wave 2 — pre-implementation design review

Review the design doc at `docs/architecture/2026-05-16-phase-4.10-function-library-wave-2.md`. This is the pre-implementation review (Phase 4.10.AA equivalent) for the function-library expansion that closes the v1 260-function target.

- Phase 4.8 SHIPPED (structured references + tables, including 4.8.G.3 calcgraph hooks closed 2026-05-16).

- Existing patterns to mirror: W5-51 (trig batch), W5-54 (lookup family), W5-55 (IFS conditional-aggregate family), W5-56 (text wave), W5-57 (math completion), W5-58 (stats family), W5-72 → W5-83 (date/time/format wave).

# Phase 4.10 — Function Library Wave 2

**Status:** Design draft, 2026-05-16. Pre-implementation. Codex pre-review pending.
**Spec ID:** FN4-260-01 (≈260-function target), FN4-260-02 (matrix-backed tests), FN4-260-03 (visible errors for unsupported).
**Effort:** 3-5 weeks per MASTER-PLAN § 4.10. This doc scopes the first ~30-50 functions across 8 batches; later waves fill the rest.

---

## 0. Context

The function library shipped 141 functions through Phase 4.5 (W5-58 closed the V1 wave 1 at 100 functions; W5-72 → W5-83 added the date/time/format wave). The compat matrix (`docs/compat/excel-matrix.md`) is **stale** — many functions tagged "Phase 4.5 deferred" actually shipped W5-72 through W5-83. The registry (`crates/ql-functions/src/registry.rs`) is the source of truth.

Wave 2 fills the remaining gaps toward the v1 260 target. This doc covers 30-50 of them; the rest are split across follow-on waves or explicitly deferred (Engineering, Database, REGEX, byte-variant TEXT).

## 1. Wave 2 scope (8 batches, ~30-50 functions total)

| Batch | Subject | Functions | Tier | Est. commits |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4.10.A | **Logical fillins** | IFS, IFNA, XOR, SWITCH | ScalarFn | 1 |
| 4.10.B | **Conditional-aggregate fillins** | MINIFS, MAXIFS, COUNTBLANK | RangeAwareFn | 1 |
| 4.10.C | **Stats `*A` variants** | AVERAGEA, MAXA, MINA | RangeAwareFn | 1 |
| 4.10.D | **Info fillins** | NA, ERROR.TYPE, ISFORMULA, TYPE | ScalarFn + ContextAwareFn (ISFORMULA) | 1 |
| 4.10.E | **Math combinatorics + sum variants** | FACT, FACTDOUBLE, COMBIN, COMBINA, PERMUT, PERMUTATIONA, SUMSQ, SUMX2MY2, SUMX2PY2, SUMXMY2 | ScalarFn / RangeAwareFn | 1-2 |
| 4.10.F | **Text utility fillins** | TEXTJOIN, VALUE, CHAR, CODE, UNICODE, UNICHAR, FIXED, DOLLAR | RangeAwareFn (TEXTJOIN) + ScalarFn | 1-2 |
| 4.10.G | **Financial basics** | PMT, FV, PV, NPER, RATE, NPV, IRR | ScalarFn / RangeAwareFn (NPV, IRR) | 2 |
| 4.10.H | **Lookup expansion** | XLOOKUP, XMATCH, OFFSET, INDIRECT, ADDRESS, COLUMN, ROW, COLUMNS, ROWS, FORMULATEXT | RangeAwareFn + ContextAwareFn | 2-3 |

Total: **~36-49 functions, 10-12 commits.** Each batch follows the W5-51 / W5-54 / W5-58 cadence: one design-batch commit + per-function tests + per-batch matrix update.

## 2. Out of scope / explicit defer

- **Engineering family** (BIN2DEC, HEX2BIN, BITAND, etc.) — `post-v1` per existing matrix.
- **Database family** (DGET, DSUM, etc.) — `defer post-v1`.
- **REGEX functions** — Google Sheets extension, not in Excel canon.
- **DBCS byte variants** (LEFTB, RIGHTB, MIDB, LENB, FINDB, SEARCHB) — UTF-16 semantics gap; bundle with the deferred UTF-16 work.
- **TEXTBEFORE / TEXTAFTER / TEXTSPLIT** — array-returning; requires 4.7 array-formula completion for non-trivial cases.
- **AGGREGATE / SUBTOTAL** — depend on 4.7 array-formula completion.
- **NETWORKDAYS.INTL / WORKDAY.INTL** — already deferred per GAP-F-09/10 (holidays + custom weekend mask).
- **CEILING.PRECISE / FLOOR.PRECISE** — already shipped CEILING.MATH/FLOOR.MATH covers most use; precise variants are aliases that need a separate one-off.

## 3. Batch 1 (4.10.A) — Logical fillins

### 3.1 Functions

#### `IFS(test1, value1, test2, value2, ..., [test_default, value_default])`

Multi-condition selector. Returns `valueK` for the first true `testK`. Excel canon:
- Variadic, even count of args (pairs of test+value).
- No `else` arg: if no test passes, return `#N/A`.
- Errors in tests propagate.
- Empty input → `#VALUE!`.
- Odd arg count → `#N/A` (matches Excel; the trailing unpaired arg is ignored as missing-value rather than syntax error).

```rust
pub fn ifs(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
    if args.is_empty() { return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value); }
    let mut i = 0;
    while i + 1 < args.len() {
        match &args[i] {
            Value::Error(e) => return Value::Error(*e),
            other => match coercion::to_logical(other) {
                Ok(true) => return args[i + 1].clone(),
                Ok(false) => {}
                Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
            },
        }
        i += 2;
    }
    Value::Error(ErrorValue::Na)
}
```

#### `IFNA(value, fallback)`

Like `IFERROR` but only catches `#N/A`. Other errors propagate.

```rust
pub fn ifna(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
    if args.len() != 2 { return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value); }
    match &args[0] {
        Value::Error(ErrorValue::Na) => args[1].clone(),
        v => v.clone(),
    }
}
```

#### `XOR(args...)`

Variadic logical XOR — true iff an odd number of args are true. Mirrors `AND`/`OR` argument handling: skip blanks, coerce non-bool via `to_logical`, propagate errors, require ≥1 non-blank.

```rust
pub fn xor(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
    if args.is_empty() { return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value); }
    let mut count = 0u64;
    let mut any_non_blank = false;
    for v in args {
        match v {
            Value::Error(e) => return Value::Error(*e),
            Value::Blank => {}
            other => {
                any_non_blank = true;
                match coercion::to_logical(other) {
                    Ok(b) => if b { count += 1 },
                    Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
                }
            }
        }
    }
    if !any_non_blank { return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value); }
    Value::Boolean(count % 2 == 1)
}
```

#### `SWITCH(expression, value1, result1, value2, result2, ..., [default])`

Matches `expression` against `valueK` (case-insensitive text equality, numeric equality, bool equality). Returns `resultK` on first match. Optional trailing `default` (odd arg count after expression).

- Variadic ≥3 args (1 expression + at least 1 value+result pair).
- Even count after expression → no default → `#N/A` on no match.
- Odd count after expression → last arg is default.
- Errors in `expression` propagate. Errors in `valueK` skip that pair (NOT propagate — matches Excel SWITCH canon, unlike IFS which propagates test errors).

```rust
pub fn switch(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
    if args.len() < 3 { return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value); }
    let expr = &args[0];
    if let Value::Error(e) = expr { return Value::Error(*e); }
    let mut i = 1;
    while i + 1 < args.len() {
        let v = &args[i];
        let r = &args[i + 1];
        if !matches!(v, Value::Error(_)) && values_match_for_switch(expr, v) {
            return r.clone();
        }
        i += 2;
    }
    // i == args.len() - 1 → unpaired trailing arg is the default.
    if i < args.len() { return args[i].clone(); }
    Value::Error(ErrorValue::Na)
}
```

`values_match_for_switch`: case-insensitive String equality, Number/Bool/Blank exact, Error never matches. Open question for Codex pre-review: does Excel allow Number↔String cross-type match (e.g., `SWITCH(1, "1", "match")`)? IronCalc reference says NO (strict type); HyperFormula says YES (coerce). Default to strict.

### 3.2 Registry additions

`registry.rs` `default_registry()`:

```rust
// Phase 4.10.A — logical fillins.
r.register("IFS", scalar_fns::ifs);
r.register("IFNA", scalar_fns::ifna);
r.register("XOR", scalar_fns::xor);
r.register("SWITCH", scalar_fns::switch);
```

### 3.3 Test plan

Per function, the existing `scalar_fns::tests` module pattern:
- Happy path (2-3 cases).
- Error propagation (test in IFS, expression in SWITCH).
- Edge cases:
  - IFS: no match → #N/A; odd arg count → #N/A; first match wins.
  - IFNA: #N/A → fallback; other errors → propagate; non-error → value.
  - XOR: 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 trues; all blanks → #VALUE!.
  - SWITCH: no match + no default → #N/A; default branch; first match wins; type-strict equality.

Target: ≥6 tests per function, ≥24 total. Workspace test count: 2643 → ~2667.

Plus 1 e2e in `workbook_runtime::tests` exercising each through the full `set_formula` path.

### 3.4 Acceptance for 4.10.A

- All 4 functions registered + scalar dispatch.
- Per-function unit tests in `scalar_fns::tests`.
- Matrix entries updated from ❌ to ✅ in `docs/compat/excel-matrix.md` § Logical.
- 7 gates green.

### 3.5 Excel canon divergences (for matrix Notes)

- **SWITCH** type-strictness: see § 3.1 open question. Default strict; document in matrix.
- **IFS** odd-arg-count → #N/A (matches Excel; the unpaired arg is silently discarded, not flagged as syntax error).
- **XOR** empty / all-blank → #VALUE! (mirrors AND/OR).

## 4. Batches 4.10.B-H — brief outlines

### 4.10.B — Conditional aggregate fillins (MINIFS, MAXIFS, COUNTBLANK)

Mirror W5-55 SUMIFS/COUNTIFS pattern. All `RangeAwareFn`. COUNTBLANK is simpler (single range, count blanks). MINIFS/MAXIFS share W5-55's criterion-predicate suite + W5-61 wildcards. **W5-60 KNOWN DIVERGENCE** applies (sum_range vs range shape) — surface in matrix Notes.

### 4.10.C — Stats `*A` variants (AVERAGEA, MAXA, MINA)

`*A` variants coerce text → 0, bool → 0/1, error propagates. Distinct from base AVERAGE/MAX/MIN which skip text+bool. RangeAwareFn.

### 4.10.D — Info fillins (NA, ERROR.TYPE, ISFORMULA, TYPE)

- `NA()` — trivial: returns `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Na)`. Scalar, no args (or 0 args).
- `ERROR.TYPE(value)` — 1=#NULL!, 2=#DIV/0!, 3=#VALUE!, 4=#REF!, 5=#NAME?, 6=#NUM!, 7=#N/A. Non-error → #N/A.
- `TYPE(value)` — 1=number, 2=text, 4=bool, 16=error, 64=array. Scalar.
- `ISFORMULA(reference)` — ContextAwareFn; needs access to `Workbook::formula_at(s,r,c)`. Reference arg semantic is tricky: today's binder passes a Value (the cell's evaluated content), not a reference handle. **Open question**: do we need a parser/binder change to preserve the reference? Probably YES — bundle with a small AST tweak. May defer to a follow-up batch if AST changes balloon scope.

### 4.10.E — Math combinatorics + sum variants

- `FACT(n)` — factorial; n<0 → #NUM!; n>170 → #NUM! (overflow).
- `FACTDOUBLE(n)` — double factorial.
- `COMBIN(n,k)`, `COMBINA(n,k)` — combinations (no rep / with rep).
- `PERMUT(n,k)`, `PERMUTATIONA(n,k)` — permutations.
- `SUMSQ`, `SUMX2MY2`, `SUMX2PY2`, `SUMXMY2` — variadic sum-of-squares + cross products. RangeAwareFn for the multi-range pair variants.

### 4.10.F — Text utility fillins

- `TEXTJOIN(delim, ignore_empty, args...)` — RangeAwareFn; supersedes CONCAT for the delimiter-controlled case.
- `VALUE(text)` — text → number, locale-aware (decimal separator). ContextAwareFn (uses locale).
- `CHAR(code)`, `CODE(text)`, `UNICODE(text)`, `UNICHAR(code)` — codepoint round-trip.
- `FIXED(number, decimals, no_commas)` — number → text with thousands grouping. Locale-aware.
- `DOLLAR(number, decimals)` — currency text. Locale-aware.

### 4.10.G — Financial basics

`PMT / FV / PV / NPER / RATE` — TVM family. Closed-form for PMT/FV/PV/NPER; RATE is iterative (Newton-Raphson with bounded iterations).

`NPV / IRR` — RangeAwareFn over cash-flow range. IRR is iterative.

Open question: which Excel sign convention to follow (inflows positive vs outflows positive)? Match Excel canon (outflows negative, signs preserved).

### 4.10.H — Lookup expansion

The big one. **XLOOKUP** is the modern replacement for VLOOKUP/HLOOKUP/INDEX+MATCH. Supports 2-way lookup, multiple match modes (exact / next-larger / next-smaller / wildcard / regex-ish), search direction (first/last). RangeAwareFn.

**XMATCH** — like XLOOKUP but returns position.

**OFFSET / INDIRECT** — return reference handles, not values. **Significant architecture impact**: currently the engine doesn't have a "reference value" tier. May require a new `Value::Reference` variant or a binder-level rewrite. **DEFER to a separate design doc** — these are not 4.10 batches, they're their own mini-phase.

**ADDRESS** — coord → A1 text. Scalar; depends on locale (R1C1 mode) — ContextAwareFn.

**COLUMN / ROW / COLUMNS / ROWS** — return position. ContextAwareFn (need owning-cell address for the 0-arg form).

**FORMULATEXT(reference)** — return formula text at reference. Same reference-tier issue as ISFORMULA — bundle with 4.10.D's ISFORMULA design.

## 5. Cross-cutting infra additions

- **Reference-tier value** (4.10.D ISFORMULA, 4.10.H OFFSET/INDIRECT/COLUMN/ROW): probably needs `FnArg::Reference { sheet, row, col }` or similar. Bundle with 4.10.D as a small AST tweak OR with 4.10.H as a larger phase. Decide based on Codex pre-review.
- **Locale-aware VALUE / FIXED / DOLLAR** (4.10.F): use existing `EvalContext.locale` (already wired in W5-153). No new infra.
- **Iterative-numeric helpers** (4.10.G RATE, IRR): a new `num_iter` module with Newton-Raphson + bounded iterations + tolerance configurable via a registry constant. Reusable.

## 6. Audit cadence (per MASTER-PLAN § 2)

- **Per batch**: self-audit at commit time. Run full 7 gates.
- **Mid-wave** (after batch D or E): Codex + Sonnet pull-up audit. Mirrors W5-58 (Phase 4.3 V1 close) + W5-62 (Phase 4.3 polish close) cadence.
- **Wave close**: closing megaudit (3-way: self + Codex + Sonnet via Agent). Mirrors W5-152 + W5-161.

## 7. Open questions for Codex pre-review

1. **SWITCH type-strictness** (§ 3.1) — IronCalc strict vs HyperFormula lenient. Default strict; verify with reference reads.
2. **Reference-tier value** (§ 5) — design footprint. Bundle 4.10.D ISFORMULA or break out as its own design doc?
3. **OFFSET/INDIRECT scope** (§ 4.10.H) — these are semantically problematic for the calcgraph (dynamic dep targets). May need explicit deferral or a constrained subset (compile-time-resolvable args only).
4. **Financial canon coverage** (§ 4.10.G) — Excel's PMT formula has multiple sign-convention modes (`type` arg = 0 or 1 for begin/end of period). Confirm we match Excel exactly.
5. **Wave-2 budget realism** — 8 batches × ~1 commit avg ≈ 8 commits, but 4.10.E (10 functions) and 4.10.H (10 functions) are large; realistic span is 10-12 commits across 2-3 sessions. Confirm or adjust.

## 8. Next action

use chrono::Datelike;

use crate::{
    calc_result::CalcResult,
    constants::{LAST_COLUMN, LAST_ROW, MAXIMUM_DATE_SERIAL_NUMBER, MINIMUM_DATE_SERIAL_NUMBER},
    expressions::{parser::Node, token::Error, types::CellReferenceIndex},
    formatter::dates::from_excel_date,
    model::Model,
};

use super::financial_util::{compute_irr, compute_npv, compute_rate, compute_xirr, compute_xnpv};

// See:
// https://github.com/apache/openoffice/blob/c014b5f2b55cff8d4b0c952d5c16d62ecde09ca1/main/scaddins/source/analysis/financial.cxx

fn is_less_than_one_year(start_date: i64, end_date: i64) -> Result<bool, String> {
    let end = from_excel_date(end_date)?;
    let start = from_excel_date(start_date)?;
    if end_date - start_date < 365 {
        return Ok(true);
    }
    let end_year = end.year();
    let start_year = start.year();
    if end_year == start_year {
        return Ok(true);
    }
    if end_year != start_year + 1 {
        return Ok(false);
    }
    let start_month = start.month();
    let end_month = end.month();
    if end_month < start_month {
        return Ok(true);
    }
    if end_month > start_month {
        return Ok(false);
    }
    // we are one year later same month
    let start_day = start.day();
    let end_day = end.day();
    Ok(end_day <= start_day)
}

fn compute_payment(
    rate: f64,
    nper: f64,
    pv: f64,
    fv: f64,
    period_start: bool,
) -> Result<f64, (Error, String)> {
    if rate == 0.0 {
        if nper == 0.0 {
            return Err((Error::NUM, "Period count must be non zero".to_string()));
        }
        return Ok(-(pv + fv) / nper);
    }
    if rate <= -1.0 {
        return Err((Error::NUM, "Rate must be > -1".to_string()));
    };
    let rate_nper = if nper == 0.0 {
        1.0
    } else {
        (1.0 + rate).powf(nper)
    };
    let result = if period_start {
        // type = 1
        (fv + pv * rate_nper) * rate / ((1.0 + rate) * (1.0 - rate_nper))
    } else {
        (fv * rate + pv * rate * rate_nper) / (1.0 - rate_nper)
    };
    if result.is_nan() || result.is_infinite() {
        return Err((Error::NUM, "Invalid result".to_string()));
    }
    Ok(result)
}

fn compute_future_value(
    rate: f64,
    nper: f64,
    pmt: f64,
    pv: f64,
    period_start: bool,
) -> Result<f64, (Error, String)> {
    if rate == 0.0 {
        return Ok(-pv - pmt * nper);
    }
    if rate == -1.0 && nper < 0.0 {
        return Err((Error::DIV, "Divide by zero".to_string()));
    }

    let rate_nper = (1.0 + rate).powf(nper);
    let fv = if period_start {
        // type = 1
        -pv * rate_nper - pmt * (1.0 + rate) * (rate_nper - 1.0) / rate
    } else {
        -pv * rate_nper - pmt * (rate_nper - 1.0) / rate
    };
    if fv.is_nan() {
        return Err((Error::NUM, "Invalid result".to_string()));
    }
    if !fv.is_finite() {
        return Err((Error::DIV, "Divide by zero".to_string()));
    }
    Ok(fv)
}

fn compute_ipmt(
    rate: f64,
    period: f64,
    period_count: f64,
    present_value: f64,
    future_value: f64,
    period_start: bool,
) -> Result<f64, (Error, String)> {
    // http://www.staff.city.ac.uk/o.s.kerr/CompMaths/WSheet4.pdf
    // https://www.experts-exchange.com/articles/1948/A-Guide-to-the-PMT-FV-IPMT-and-PPMT-Functions.html
    // type = 0 (end of period)
    // impt = -[(1+rate)^(period-1)*(pv*rate+pmt)-pmt]
    // ipmt = FV(rate, period-1, payment, pv, type) * rate
    // type = 1 (beginning of period)
    // ipmt = (FV(rate, period-2, payment, pv, type) - payment) * rate
    let payment = compute_payment(
        rate,
        period_count,
        present_value,
        future_value,
        period_start,
    )?;
    if period < 1.0 || period >= period_count + 1.0 {
        return Err((
            Error::NUM,
            format!("Period must be between 1 and {}", period_count + 1.0),
        ));
    }
    if period == 1.0 && period_start {
        Ok(0.0)
    } else {
        let p = if period_start {
            period - 2.0
        } else {
            period - 1.0
        };
        let c = if period_start { -payment } else { 0.0 };
        let fv = compute_future_value(rate, p, payment, present_value, period_start)?;
        Ok((fv + c) * rate)
    }
}

fn compute_ppmt(
    rate: f64,
    period: f64,
    period_count: f64,
    present_value: f64,
    future_value: f64,
    period_start: bool,
) -> Result<f64, (Error, String)> {
    let payment = compute_payment(
        rate,
        period_count,
        present_value,
        future_value,
        period_start,
    )?;
    // It's a bit unfortunate that the first thing compute_ipmt does is compute_payment again
    let ipmt = compute_ipmt(
        rate,
        period,
        period_count,
        present_value,
        future_value,
        period_start,
    )?;
    Ok(payment - ipmt)
}

// These formulas revolve around compound interest and annuities.
// The financial functions pv, rate, nper, pmt and fv:
// rate = interest rate per period
// nper (number of periods) = loan term
// pv (present value) = loan amount
// fv (future value) = cash balance after last payment. Default is 0
// type = the annuity type indicates when payments are due
//         * 0 (default) Payments are made at the end of the period
//         * 1 Payments are made at the beginning of the period (like a lease or rent)
// The variable period_start is true if type is 1
// They are linked by the formulas:
// If rate != 0
//   $pv*(1+rate)^nper+pmt*(1+rate*type)*((1+rate)^nper-1)/rate+fv=0$
// If rate = 0
//   $pmt*nper+pv+fv=0$
// All, except for rate are easily solvable in terms of the others.
// In these formulas the payment (pmt) is normally negative

impl<'a> Model<'a> {
    fn get_array_of_numbers_generic(
        &mut self,
        arg: &Node,
        cell: &CellReferenceIndex,
        accept_number_node: bool,
        handle_empty_cell: impl Fn() -> Result<Option<f64>, CalcResult>,
        handle_non_number_cell: impl Fn() -> Result<Option<f64>, CalcResult>,
    ) -> Result<Vec<f64>, CalcResult> {
        let mut values = Vec::new();
        match self.evaluate_node_in_context(arg, *cell) {
            CalcResult::Number(value) if accept_number_node => values.push(value),
            CalcResult::Number(_) => {
                return Err(CalcResult::new_error(
                    Error::VALUE,
                    *cell,
                    "Expected range of numbers".to_string(),
                ));
            }
            CalcResult::Range { left, right } => {
                if left.sheet != right.sheet {
                    return Err(CalcResult::new_error(
                        Error::VALUE,
                        *cell,
                        "Ranges are in different sheets".to_string(),
                    ));
                }
                let sheet = left.sheet;
                let row1 = left.row;
                let mut row2 = right.row;
                let column1 = left.column;
                let mut column2 = right.column;
                if row1 == 1 && row2 == LAST_ROW {
                    row2 = self
                        .workbook
                        .worksheet(sheet)
                        .map_err(|_| {
                            CalcResult::new_error(
                                Error::ERROR,
                                *cell,
                                format!("Invalid worksheet index: '{sheet}'"),
                            )
                        })?
                        .dimension()
                        .max_row;
                }
                if column1 == 1 && column2 == LAST_COLUMN {
                    column2 = self
                        .workbook
                        .worksheet(sheet)
                        .map_err(|_| {
                            CalcResult::new_error(
                                Error::ERROR,
                                *cell,
                                format!("Invalid worksheet index: '{sheet}'"),
                            )
                        })?
                        .dimension()
                        .max_column;
                }
                for row in row1..=row2 {
                    for column in column1..=column2 {
                        let cell_ref = CellReferenceIndex { sheet, row, column };
                        match self.evaluate_cell(cell_ref) {
                            CalcResult::Number(value) => values.push(value),
                            error @ CalcResult::Error { .. } => return Err(error),
                            CalcResult::EmptyCell => {
                                if let Some(value) = handle_empty_cell()? {
                                    values.push(value);
                                }
                            }
                            _ => {
                                if let Some(value) = handle_non_number_cell()? {
                                    values.push(value);
                                }
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            error @ CalcResult::Error { .. } => return Err(error),
            _ => {
                handle_non_number_cell()?;
            }
        }
        Ok(values)
    }

./docs/known-gaps.md:76:| GAP-F-06 | `COUNT` provenance-aware semantics not implemented. Excel canon: `COUNT(TRUE, "1")` direct args = 2 (counts logical + numeric text); `COUNT(A1:A2)` over the same cells = 0 (skips them as range/ref). Quantbook's eval dispatcher pre-evaluates all args before calling the function body, so `count()` cannot distinguish provenance — always skips text/bool. Filed W5-67 closure (Codex Phase 4.4 mega-audit HIGH 1). Implementing this needs an `ExprPlan`-level marker propagated to the function dispatch (separate from the existing `is_aggregate_function` / RangeAwareFn machinery). | `crates/ql-functions/src/scalar_fns.rs::count` matches only `Value::Number`; pinned by `count_direct_bool_and_numeric_text_v1_divergence` | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 (function library wave 2) or earlier if real-world workbooks surface it |
./docs/known-gaps.md:77:| GAP-F-08 | `DAYS` text-arg parsing not implemented. Excel canon: `DAYS("2024-12-31", "2024-01-01")` parses each text arg via DATEVALUE first. Quantbook V1 rejects text args with `#VALUE!` (strict-numeric scalar fn). Users should call `DATEVALUE` explicitly. Filed W5-74. | `crates/ql-functions/src/date_fns.rs::days` strict numeric; pinned by `days_text_arg_is_value_error_v1_divergence` | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 polish |
./docs/known-gaps.md:78:| GAP-F-09 | `NETWORKDAYS` holidays-array arg not supported in V1. Excel canon: 3rd arg is an optional range of holiday dates that are subtracted from the working-day count. Quantbook's `ContextAwareFn` tier signature (`fn(&[Value], &EvalContext) -> Value`) has pre-evaluated scalar args only — range args silently lose shape (or surface as `#CALC!`). Supporting holidays needs a 4th registry tier `RangeAndContextAwareFn = fn(&[FnArg], &EvalContext) -> Value`. V1: 3-arg call returns `#VALUE!`. | `crates/ql-functions/src/date_fns.rs::networkdays_ctx`; pinned by `networkdays_with_holidays_arg_is_value_error_v1_divergence` | Engine | Engine Phase 4.5.C (when tier-4 lands) or 4.7 array formulas trigger |
./docs/known-gaps.md:80:| GAP-F-11 | `YEARFRAC` basis 0 (US NASD 30/360) uses the European 30/360 algorithm (basis 4) in V1. The US NASD convention has special-casing for end-of-month days (Feb 28/29, day-31 treated as day-30 in specific cases) that adds ~50 LOC of edge-case logic. V1: basis 0 returns the European 30/360 result. Real-world divergence is 0-1 days out of 360 for most date ranges; financial applications using basis 0 specifically may need the proper algorithm. | `crates/ql-functions/src/date_fns.rs::yearfrac_ctx` basis-0 branch falls through to basis-4 algorithm | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 polish |
./docs/known-gaps.md:81:| GAP-F-07 | `FormatTable` GC / compaction not implemented. Sparse format-overlay (Phase 4.5.D) interns format strings into a workbook-level `FormatTable`. When cells change format, the old `FormatId` entry is NOT garbage-collected. Long-lived workbooks that cycle through many formats grow the table. **Decision (W5-68 design doc § 7.5):** deferred — typical workbooks don't cycle formats; a compaction pass lands in Phase 4.10 or on explicit user request. | Filed at design time (W5-68); no code yet | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 |
./docs/known-gaps.md:82:| GAP-T-01 | Time-zone offset acquisition for `NOW()` / `TODAY()` local-time policy not implemented. Phase 4.5.A.0 EvalContext design includes a `NowProvider` trait with `utc_offset_seconds()`. The native implementation needs either a small `iana_time_zone` crate dep (~50 LOC, single-purpose) or a libc FFI `localtime_r` call. **W5-76 mega-audit closure:** `NowProvider::System` ships returning UTC offset 0 — `NOW()` / `TODAY()` are regression-neutral with the pre-W5-71 UTC path. The proper local-time hook is rescheduled from "immediate" to **Phase 4.10 polish** alongside the function-library wave 2. WASM target uses a host callback (Phase 6.3). | Filed W5-68; partial-ship in W5-71 (UTC parity); status reaffirmed W5-76 | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 polish (was 4.5.A) |
./docs/known-gaps.md:83:| GAP-F-12 | `WorkbookRuntime::format_cache` can stale if `Workbook::formats_mut()` mutates the FormatTable AFTER `read_display` has cached the General fallback for a (then-unknown) id. The cache is keyed by `FormatId`; cache entries stay correct as long as mutations go through `WorkbookRuntime::intern_format` (the documented append-before-mutate path). Direct low-level mutation through `formats_mut()` (loader / tests only) on a live runtime is the pathological sequence. Analogous to the `WorkbookEnv` date-system staleness caveat documented at `Workbook::set_date_system`. **Decision (W5-84):** documented in the `read_display` docstring; cache-invalidation API deferred until profiling shows a real-world hit. | Filed W5-84 mega-audit closure (Codex MEDIUM-2) | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 polish or earlier if IDE forces it |
./docs/known-gaps.md:106:| GAP-G-05 | `formula_to_stripe_keys` (the W5-50 reverse index added to `StripeIndex` for revocation) has NOT been benchmarked at scale. For a workbook with N formulas each registering M range deps spanning K stripe keys per range, memory grows as `O(N · M · K)` — bounded but unbenchmarked. Codex W5-49 listed this as a stop-condition for Option A. Acceptance: bench fixture in `crates/ql-bench` measuring memory growth as a function of N for N ∈ {1k, 10k, 100k} formulas with realistic range-dep distributions. | `crates/ql-calcgraph/src/stripes.rs` `formula_to_stripe_keys: HashMap<NodeId, Vec<StripeKey>>` field has no bench coverage | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 (function library wave 2) or earlier if scale workload surfaces it |

./docs/phase0/references-reading-log.md:5:The spec's Week 1 Days 5-7 deliverable was deep reading of the reference engines that inform Quantbook's design. R1-R8 of the recovery sprint covered immediate audit findings but didn't have the multi-hour focus block needed for the reading. This document captures the deep-read sprint output: per-section findings with file:line citations, the populated adoption matrix, and the plan corrections the reading surfaced.
./docs/phase0/references-reading-log.md:17:**Provenance:** anything LIFTED into Quantbook code with non-trivial inspiration from a reference gets a per-file entry under `docs/legal/<source>-provenance.md` (gitignored per CORR-10 until ship-prep).
./docs/phase0/references-reading-log.md:160:For Quantbook: this **IS** a useful pattern for the kernel hot path — a borrowed chunk view with optional validity mask hands directly to SIMD code. Worth lifting for `ql-exec` (Week 4) where SIMD kernels need a uniform borrowed-chunk type that works for both base Arrow arrays and overlay-merged chunks.

Evaluator's `processVertexOnCycle` (`Evaluator.ts:102-112`) sets `CellError(ErrorType.CYCLE, undefined, vertex)`. **No iteration limit, no fixpoint.** Matches Quantbook Phase 0 design.

./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-61-megaudit-sonnet.md:37:Excel itself has bounded-time matching here (Excel's matcher is iterative); this divergence is invisible until someone actually exploits it. Filing as HIGH because (i) it's an unbounded-runtime path triggered by ordinary-looking user input, (ii) the W5-61 commit ships it as "production-ready Excel-canon wildcards" with no perf disclaimer, and (iii) Quantbook's larger story is "1M-row sheets that recompute fast" — exponential matching breaks that story silently.
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-61-megaudit-sonnet.md:81:The brief's CONCERN-G ("predicate over-routing") spoiler says `"file_path~temp.txt"` triggers the wildcard path but still matches correctly. I walked through the routing manually: `has_wildcards("foo~bar")` returns false (consumes `~b` as escape), but `rest.contains('~')` is true, so `build_predicate` routes to `Predicate::TextWildcard(Eq, compile("foo~bar"))`. `compile` then treats `~b` as the lenient case (peek not in `?/*/~`, so `~` becomes a literal — line 58 of `wildcard.rs`), and the pattern is `[Literal("FOO~BAR")]`. Apply on `Value::Text("foo~bar")`: matches. Apply on `Value::Number(5.0)`: returns false. **Correct in both cases**, but the divergence-from-Predicate::Text on Number cells (TextWildcard never matches non-Text; Predicate::Text DOES try numeric parse) is subtle and not pinned by any test.
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-61-megaudit-sonnet.md:83:I also confirmed `"foo~"` (trailing tilde, no escape target) compiles correctly to `[Literal("FOO~")]` and matches the cell `"foo~"`. CONCERN-A asked about this specifically; not pinned by a test.
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-61-megaudit-sonnet.md:135:For `mode = NaN`: `NaN == 0.0` is false → falls into the mode≠0 branch. Excel would probably return `#VALUE!` for NaN mode. Quantbook treats NaN-mode as truthy and continues. Not breaking, not pinned.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:18:## 1. Goals (Quantbook UX preferences, not Excel file semantics)

./docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:23:2. **Locale-aware separators** — argument separator, decimal separator, AND array literal separators (Phase 4.7 ships array literals; locale must cover them). Quantbook UX preference; storage stays canonical English.

Note: rule 7 (function-returns-array → top-left) deferred — array-returning functions in scalar context still return `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit; closing megaudit (4.9.O) decides whether to bundle that with cell-boundary spill rewiring or defer to a Phase 4.10 polish item. |

Reference arg semantic is tricky: today's binder passes a Value (the cell's evaluated content), not a reference handle. **Open question**: do we need a parser/binder change to preserve the reference? Probably YES — bundle with a small AST tweak. May defer to a follow-up batch if AST changes balloon scope.

./docs/architecture/2026-05-16-phase-4.10-function-library-wave-2.md:257:4. **Financial canon coverage** (§ 4.10.G) — Excel's PMT formula has multiple sign-convention modes (`type` arg = 0 or 1 for begin/end of period). Confirm we match Excel exactly.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-16-phase-4.10-function-library-wave-2.md:258:5. **Wave-2 budget realism** — 8 batches × ~1 commit avg ≈ 8 commits, but 4.10.E (10 functions) and 4.10.H (10 functions) are large; realistic span is 10-12 commits across 2-3 sessions. Confirm or adjust.

./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-coercion-matrix.md:10:- `[CURRENT]` — Current Quantbook behavior. Verified in code as of W5-62. May be CANON or DIVERGENCE.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-coercion-matrix.md:88:| Range-aggregate cell | strict           | **skip**       | →#VALUE! (Quantbook strict; Excel skips) | propagate      |
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-coercion-matrix.md:94:The **Range-aggregate cell** row is the documented Quantbook divergence from Excel (matrix item 4). Excel's SUM(A1:A10) SKIPS text cells; Quantbook returns #VALUE!. This is intentional V1 behavior pinned by tests.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-coercion-matrix.md:142:| `TYPE(v)` (Excel)    | Returns 1/2/4/8/16/64 for type identification. **NOT REGISTERED** in Quantbook V1. | [FUTURE] |
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-coercion-matrix.md:143:| `NA()` (Excel)       | Returns `#N/A`. **NOT REGISTERED** in Quantbook V1.                 | [FUTURE] |
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-coercion-matrix.md:144:| `ERROR.TYPE(v)` (Excel) | Returns 1-7 for error type. **NOT REGISTERED** in Quantbook V1.  | [FUTURE] |
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-coercion-matrix.md:145:| `AGGREGATE/SUBTOTAL` | Ignore-errors options. **NOT REGISTERED** in Quantbook V1.          | [FUTURE Phase 4.10] |
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-coercion-matrix.md:180:- **Excel's "skip text and blanks in range" canon for SUM/AVERAGE/MIN/MAX**. Documented Quantbook V1 divergence — current behavior propagates `#VALUE!` for any text cell in a range arg (via `coerce_numeric` → `to_number_strict`), while Excel SILENTLY SKIPS text cells in range positions. ERROR cells in range correctly propagate in BOTH Excel and Quantbook — that's not the divergence. Codex review flagged the previous wording in this section was misframed; corrected. Defending V1 behavior: strict-text-in-range is easier for users to debug (no silent type-confusion bug masking), at the cost of one-step-extra to use `=SUMIF(A:A, ">0")` instead of `=SUM(A:A)` over text-bearing ranges.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-coercion-matrix.md:205:| Matrix tests over-pin Quantbook-specific divergences as canon           | Each divergence is doc-tagged; test name includes "divergence". |
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-coercion-matrix.md:240:- **HIGH 2 fix**: SUM/AVERAGE non-goal wording corrected — the divergence is TEXT-in-range, not errors-in-range. Errors propagate correctly in both Excel and Quantbook for range args.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-coercion-matrix.md:241:- **HIGH 3 fix**: §3.4 override matrix expanded with COUNT, COUNTA (these diverge from each other on error handling), ISNUMBER/ISTEXT/ISBLANK/ISLOGICAL, CHOOSE, AVERAGE/MIN/MAX/MEDIAN/MODE empty-range cases, LARGE/SMALL strict-range, RANK/RANK.EQ/RANK.AVG. Marked TYPE/NA/ERROR.TYPE as NOT REGISTERED.

./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-49-50-51-megaudit-prompt.md:1:# Deep mega-audit — W5-49 + W5-50 + W5-51 (Quantbook engine)
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-49-50-51-megaudit-prompt.md:3:You are auditing three commits that landed in one session on the Quantbook spreadsheet engine. Branch `feat/quantbook-engine`. Repo at `/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/`. The session shipped a correctness fix that the operator/architect (Claude Opus 4.7) made on their own; this audit is the independent double-check pass.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:80:| `serial_to_ymd(60.0, Excel1900)` | `Ok((1900, 2, 29))` | The "phantom" leap day. IronCalc maps through `chrono::NaiveDate` which can't represent this; Quantbook returns it explicitly via the bug branch. |
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:91:These rows are pinned by tests in sub-phase 4.5.A and 4.5.B. IronCalc is NOT a clean oracle here because it routes through `chrono::NaiveDate` which can't represent 1900-02-29; Quantbook's roll-our-own date module has the explicit bug-branch advantage.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:95:Excel canon: `NOW()` and `TODAY()` return the LOCAL date/time of the user's machine (no time zones modeled in the serial — the value IS local). Pre-W5-68 Quantbook used `SystemTime::now().duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH).as_secs()` which gives UTC seconds.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:201:| `SECOND(serial)` | Returns 0..59 (Excel canon — no fractional seconds). NOW() input is whole-second from `Duration::as_secs()`; sub-second precision is a **non-goal** for V1 (Codex LOW 2 pin) — if Phase 4.10+ adds sub-second NOW, SECOND() truncates to match Excel's integer return. |
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:308:The original draft said "each cell can carry `format_id: Option<FormatId>`" — but Quantbook storage is **columnar** (Arrow chunks + sparse overlays at `crates/ql-storage/src/column.rs`), not row-based with per-cell structs. There's no `Cell` type to attach `format_id` to. Concrete redesign:
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:363:### 7.5 Compaction (deferred to Phase 4.10)
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:365:When cells change format, the old FormatId entry isn't garbage-collected. A workbook that cycles through many formats could grow the table. **Decision:** deferred compaction pass (Phase 4.10 or runtime trigger) — typical workbooks don't cycle formats. File as a known gap.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:418:- **Fraction format** (`# ?/?`) — Phase 4.10.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:420:- **CRLF / Windows-vs-Unix line endings in text formats** — Phase 4.10.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:435:| **IronCalc reference can't represent serial 60** (Codex MEDIUM 1) | Quantbook tests pin serial-60 behavior independently of IronCalc; reference is consulted for the non-buggy serials only |
./docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:491:- Function library scope — are 25 functions the right cut for Phase 4.5, or should ISOWEEKNUM / WORKDAY.INTL split to 4.10?
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:86:All rows must have the same number of cells. Mismatched-row literals (e.g. `{1, 2; 3}`) error at parse time with `ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected, found, row }`. **This is a Quantbook v1 subset** — Excel pads with `#N/A` for missing cells, and `{1,2;3}` evaluates as `{1,2;3,#N/A}`. Quantbook v1 chooses loud rejection over silent padding; the pad-with-`#N/A` variant is a Phase 4.10 polish item gated on test-corpus completeness.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:95:- `error literal` — `#NULL!`, `#DIV/0!`, `#VALUE!`, `#REF!`, `#NAME?`, `#NUM!`, `#N/A`, `#SPILL!`, `#CALC!` — the nine Excel-canon error sigils available as `ErrorValue::ALL` entries in `ql-types`. Needed for `FILTER(_, _, #N/A)` etc. (W5-97 closure / Sonnet M2: `#GETTING_DATA` was listed in the original draft but is not in `ErrorValue::ALL` — it's an Excel-Online-specific volatile data-wait state that we don't model in v1; lifting it would require adding the variant alongside `Disconnected`/`Timeout`. Deferred to Phase 4.10 or whenever a real use case appears.)
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:97:NO nested CellRef / RangeRef / Function calls / Unary operators (other than the implicit sign on numeric literals). Codex MEDIUM finding: explicitly document this as a Quantbook v1 subset.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:99:Phase 4.10 lifts to allow refs (`{A1, B1; A2, B2}`) once the array evaluator and dependency extraction prove stable.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:602:**v1 scope (post-W5-108 / Phase 4.7.O closure update):** 1D inputs only — `array` and `include` must both be row vectors (1×N) or both column vectors (N×1), with matching length. 2D inputs (N×M array + N×1 or 1×M mask) deferred to v2 / Phase 4.10. The shipped implementation at `crates/ql-functions/src/array_returning_fns.rs:335-341` rejects any `array` with `rows > 1 AND cols > 1` with `#VALUE!`. The 2D enumeration below describes the eventual target shape; do NOT take it as the shipped 4.7 scope.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:607:  - If `array` is N×M and `include` is N×1: filter rows where include[i]=TRUE. **(v2 / Phase 4.10)**
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:608:  - If `array` is N×M and `include` is 1×M: filter columns where include[j]=TRUE. **(v2 / Phase 4.10)**
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:609:  - If `include` is N×M (same shape as array): cell-wise filter — Phase 4.10 (out of scope for 4.7).
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:637:### 13.4 Deferred for Phase 4.10
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:665:Excel 365+ dynamic arrays canonicalize "array in non-array context → spill or `@`". Legacy Excel canonicalizes "array in scalar context → implicit intersection with caller's row/col". Quantbook v1 Phase 4.7 chooses neither: array-in-scalar-context → `#CALC!` (§ 6.3). This is a defensible safe default; Phase 4.9 lights up `@` syntax + an `xls_calc` mode toggle.
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:677:- Array constants with cell refs inside: `{A1, B1; A2, B2}` (Phase 4.10).
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:679:- Array operators (`A1:A10 + 1` broadcasting) (Phase 4.10 — needs separate "scalar broadcast across array" semantics).
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:680:- UNIQUE / SORT / SORTBY / RANDARRAY (Phase 4.10).
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:683:- N/A padding for arity-mismatched literals (Phase 4.10 polish).
./docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:702:   **A:** Constants + error literals only in v1. Refs deferred to Phase 4.10 (§ 3.3).

Excel rejects with `#NAME?`. Quantbook accepts; the binder typically rejects with `UnknownSheet`. Phase 4.6.E audit LOW. | `ql-formula-syntax/src/lexer.rs::try_lex_sheet_name_prefix` doesn't reject A1-shape; `Sheet1!A1` semantics. | Engine | Engine Phase 4.9 (or sooner if a clean test fails) |
./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:1601:docs/known-gaps.md:81:| GAP-F-07 | `FormatTable` GC / compaction not implemented. Sparse format-overlay (Phase 4.5.D) interns format strings into a workbook-level `FormatTable`. When cells change format, the old `FormatId` entry is NOT garbage-collected. Long-lived workbooks that cycle through many formats grow the table. **Decision (W5-68 design doc § 7.5):** deferred — typical workbooks don't cycle formats; a compaction pass lands in Phase 4.10 or on explicit user request. | Filed at design time (W5-68); no code yet | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 |
./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:1602:docs/known-gaps.md:83:| GAP-F-12 | `WorkbookRuntime::format_cache` can stale if `Workbook::formats_mut()` mutates the FormatTable AFTER `read_display` has cached the General fallback for a (then-unknown) id. The cache is keyed by `FormatId`; cache entries stay correct as long as mutations go through `WorkbookRuntime::intern_format` (the documented append-before-mutate path). Direct low-level mutation through `formats_mut()` (loader / tests only) on a live runtime is the pathological sequence. Analogous to the `WorkbookEnv` date-system staleness caveat documented at `Workbook::set_date_system`. **Decision (W5-84):** documented in the `read_display` docstring; cache-invalidation API deferred until profiling shows a real-world hit. | Filed W5-84 mega-audit closure (Codex MEDIUM-2) | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 polish or earlier if IDE forces it |
./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:1615:docs/phase0/references-reading-log.md:494:| **`NumericChunk<'a>` borrowed view** | Formualizer `stripes.rs:3-21` | `pub struct NumericChunk<'a> { data: &'a [f64], validity: Option<ValidityMask> }` for SIMD kernels | Adapt to Quantbook's Value type | (Phase 0: defer to Week 4 ql-exec) |
./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:1718:docs/architecture/2026-05-15-r1c1-locales-implicit-intersection.md:186:| 12 | **4.9.L** (W5-148, shipped) | Round-trip + edge case verification suite. +12 end-to-end property tests in `workbook_runtime::tests`: mixed-relativity range rejection at set_formula, `Sales[@Col]` vs `@Sales[Qty]` semantic equivalence, `@A:A` / `@1:1` whole-range narrowing at anchor, `Sheet1!@A1` cross-sheet, R1C1+EN round-trip through op-log replay (set_reference_mode + set_formula both replayed), DE+EN round-trip through replay, `@` preservation through replay, reference_mode/locale changes leave stored text canonical, `@scalar` idempotency, `@SUM(scalar args)` no-op. Note: rule 7 (function-returns-array → top-left) deferred — array-returning functions in scalar context still return `#CALC!` per existing 4.7.G limit; closing megaudit (4.9.O) decides whether to bundle that with cell-boundary spill rewiring or defer to a Phase 4.10 polish item. |

./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:1802:docs/architecture/2026-05-13-cross-sheet-references.md:137:- Reserved sheet names (`AI`, future): treated as plain SheetName at lex time; binder rejects with `BindError::ReservedSheetName` if needed.
./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:1825:docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md:602:**v1 scope (post-W5-108 / Phase 4.7.O closure update):** 1D inputs only — `array` and `include` must both be row vectors (1×N) or both column vectors (N×1), with matching length. 2D inputs (N×M array + N×1 or 1×M mask) deferred to v2 / Phase 4.10. The shipped implementation at `crates/ql-functions/src/array_returning_fns.rs:335-341` rejects any `array` with `rows > 1 AND cols > 1` with `#VALUE!`. The 2D enumeration below describes the eventual target shape; do NOT take it as the shipped 4.7 scope.

./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:2166:docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:365:When cells change format, the old FormatId entry isn't garbage-collected. ./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:2173:docs/phase2/exit-packet.md:12:This packet closes Phase 2A of the Quantbook engine. Phase 2A shipped five feature items (2A.1, 2A.2, 2A.4, 2A.5, and the deferred-from-Phase-1 H6 work in 2A.7) plus a complete megaudit cycle (2A.6 + 2A.7-.13) — the audit's findings drove most of the substantive correctness work.

Provenance-aware dispatch is deferred. | 🟢 errors / 🟡 direct-args | `count_skips_errors_canon`, `count_skips_text_and_bools_v1_divergence`, `count_direct_bool_and_numeric_text_v1_divergence` |

./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:2268:docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.6-closing-megaudit.md:91:**Status:** Excel rejects `A1!B2` as `#NAME?`. ./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:2271:docs/audits/2026-05-13-phase-4.5-megaudit-sonnet.md:171:365.25 divisor; full Excel basis-1 algorithm deferred to Phase 4.10."
./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:2288:docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:123:| CEILING.MATH | ✅ | 7 | 4.3 polish | W5-61: signature `(number, [significance], [mode])`. Significance is `abs(significance)` — sign ignored (unlike CEILING). Default significance=1, mode=0. Positive number always rounds toward +∞; negative number with mode=0 rounds toward +∞ (toward zero), with mode≠0 rounds toward -∞ (away from zero). significance=0 returns 0. CEILING.PRECISE deferred to 4.10. |
./docs/audits/2026-05-16-phase-4.8.G.3-closing-megaudit-codex.md:2289:docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:124:| FLOOR.MATH | ✅ | 8 | 4.3 polish | W5-61: signature `(number, [significance], [mode])`. Mirror of CEILING.MATH. Positive number rounds toward -∞; negative+mode=0 toward -∞ (away from zero); negative+mode≠0 toward +∞ (toward zero). significance=0 + nonzero number → #DIV/0! (mirrors FLOOR canon, diverges from CEILING.MATH). FLOOR.PRECISE deferred to 4.10. |

./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-68-codex-dates-prompt.md:46:2. The bug specifically: in 1900 system, serial 60 = "1900-02-29". For dates `>= 1900-03-01`, the bug just shifts the offset by 1. For dates `<= 1900-02-28`, the bug is invisible. The actual divergence is `DATEVALUE("1900-02-29")` should return serial 60 in 1900 system (Quantbook + Excel), but `DATE(1900, 2, 29)` could either return 60 (Excel canon) or `#NUM!` (logical). What's the right call? Doc doesn't pin this.
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-68-codex-dates-prompt.md:58:Estimate: 15 fns × ~50 LOC = 750 LOC. Is the LOC estimate realistic for the V1 wave? Cross-check with IronCalc's 1703 LOC for 18 fns (their fns are more elaborate; would Quantbook's match scope?).
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-68-codex-dates-prompt.md:96:1. Most Excel cells DON'T carry a custom format — they use the "General" default which is type-context-dependent (date if serial looks like one, number otherwise). The doc punts on the "looks like a date" heuristic. What is it actually? In Excel, the user has to apply a date format explicitly — General mode keeps the serial visible. Is that what Quantbook does, or should we auto-detect?
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-68-codex-dates-prompt.md:105:1. The upgrade is a behavior change: pre-W5-68, `NOW()` returned the "approximate" Excel-canon serial; post-W5-68, it returns the EXACT Excel serial. For dates `>= 1900-03-01` this is identical. For dates `< 1900-03-01` it's different. Quantbook NOW always returns current date (post-2024), so no user impact — but the contract change should be noted.
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-68-codex-dates-prompt.md:107:3. NOW() returns time-of-day as the fractional part. Excel does the same. But Quantbook's `set_test_now_secs(0)` would give exact `25569.0`; with the leap-year-aware conversion, is that still `25569.0` or shifted?

./docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:104:## 1. Goals (Quantbook UX preferences, not Excel file semantics)
./docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:108:1. **R1C1 reference syntax** as an alternative display + input mode. Toggleable per workbook (a Quantbook UX preference — not stored in Excel files, which are mode-neutral). Storage canon stays A1.
./docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex-retry.md:109:2. **Locale-aware separators** — argument separator, decimal separator, AND array literal separators (Phase 4.7 ships array literals; locale must cover them). Quantbook UX preference; storage stays canonical English.

./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.B-codex-review.txt:142:All rows must have the same number of cells. Mismatched-row literals (e.g. `{1, 2; 3}`) error at parse time with `ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected, found, row }`. **This is a Quantbook v1 subset** — Excel pads with `#N/A` for missing cells, and `{1,2;3}` evaluates as `{1,2;3,#N/A}`. Quantbook v1 chooses loud rejection over silent padding; the pad-with-`#N/A` variant is a Phase 4.10 polish item gated on test-corpus completeness.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.B-codex-review.txt:153:NO nested CellRef / RangeRef / Function calls / Unary operators (other than the implicit sign on numeric literals). Codex MEDIUM finding: explicitly document this as a Quantbook v1 subset.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.B-codex-review.txt:155:Phase 4.10 lifts to allow refs (`{A1, B1; A2, B2}`) once the array evaluator and dependency extraction prove stable.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.B-codex-review.txt:320:This is the Phase 4.7 design doc (Array Formulas + Dynamic Spills) for the Quantbook engine. It is the spec for ~13 sub-phases of implementation work. You are being asked for an independent design review BEFORE any code ships.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.B-codex-review.txt:414:All rows must have the same number of cells. Mismatched-row literals (e.g. `{1, 2; 3}`) error at parse time with `ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected, found, row }`. Matches Excel canon: Excel pads with `#N/A` but for our v1 we reject loudly — pad-with-N/A is a Phase 4.10 polish item.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.B-codex-review.txt:418:Array cells in v1: NUMBER / STRING / BOOL / unary-minus-NUMBER only. NO nested CellRef / RangeRef / Function calls inside an array literal. This matches IronCalc's `ArrayNode` model (numbers/strings/booleans/errors). Phase 4.10 can lift to allow `{A1, B1; A2, B2}` if needed.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.B-codex-review.txt:471:Registration: `register_array_fn(name, sig, f)`. Dispatched by name like the other tiers. Functions that ALWAYS return arrays (SEQUENCE, FILTER, UNIQUE, SORT, TRANSPOSE) register as `ArrayFn`. Functions that CAN return arrays based on input (e.g. an arithmetic op on arrays — Phase 4.7 doesn't do this; that's array-of-scalars broadcasting, Phase 4.10) stay scalar.

./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.B-codex-review.txt:4057:   559	Excel 365+ dynamic arrays canonicalize "array in non-array context → spill or `@`". Legacy Excel canonicalizes "array in scalar context → implicit intersection with caller's row/col". Quantbook v1 Phase 4.7 chooses neither: array-in-scalar-context → `#CALC!` (§ 6.3). This is a defensible safe default; Phase 4.9 lights up `@` syntax + an `xls_calc` mode toggle.

./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.C-sonnet-review.md:65:### 4.1 Quantbook-specific sigils not per-tested
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.C-sonnet-review.md:69:The commit adds 10 per-sigil tests covering all 9 Excel-canonical sigils plus `#CIRC!`. The 5 Quantbook-specific sigils have **no dedicated positive lex tests**:
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.C-sonnet-review.md:79:**Severity:** MEDIUM — the code is correct today; this is an under-tested area for Quantbook-specific surface. A test for at least `#DISCONNECTED!` and `#AI_NOT_AVAILABLE_V1` (the longest sigil, no `!`/`?` terminator) would close the gap.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.C-sonnet-review.md:141:**How this manifests:** A new `ErrorValue::Foo` added to `sigil()` but not `ALL` would compile, pass all existing tests, and silently reject `#FOO!` as `InvalidErrorSigil` in the lexer.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.C-sonnet-review.md:172:Five Quantbook-specific error sigils (`#DISCONNECTED!`, `#BINDING!`, `#TIMEOUT!`, `#PERMISSION!`, `#AI_NOT_AVAILABLE_V1`) have no positive lex tests. The code is correct today but the gap would let a future regression in `ErrorValue::ALL` maintenance go undetected.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.C-sonnet-review.md:178:- Either add `ErrorValue::GettingData` with sigil `"#GETTING_DATA"` (+ ALL entry + lex test), or strike `#GETTING_DATA` from § 3.3 and note it as "deferred / Excel-Online-only, not in Quantbook v1".

./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:49:7. walk_plan_for_deps treats ExprPlan::Array as no-deps. Per design § 3.3 array cells are restricted to literals, so this is correct today. But will it be correct after Phase 4.10 lifts the restriction to allow refs inside array literals? File as a known-gap or note?
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:170:95:- `error literal` — `#NULL!`, `#DIV/0!`, `#VALUE!`, `#REF!`, `#NAME?`, `#NUM!`, `#N/A`, `#SPILL!`, `#CALC!` — the nine Excel-canon error sigils available as `ErrorValue::ALL` entries in `ql-types`. Needed for `FILTER(_, _, #N/A)` etc. (W5-97 closure / Sonnet M2: `#GETTING_DATA` was listed in the original draft but is not in `ErrorValue::ALL` — it's an Excel-Online-specific volatile data-wait state that we don't model in v1; lifting it would require adding the variant alongside `Disconnected`/`Timeout`. Deferred to Phase 4.10 or whenever a real use case appears.)
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:171:99:Phase 4.10 lifts to allow refs (`{A1, B1; A2, B2}`) once the array evaluator and dependency extraction prove stable.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:202:528:  - If `include` is N×M (same shape as array): cell-wise filter — Phase 4.10 (out of scope for 4.7).
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:211:584:Excel 365+ dynamic arrays canonicalize "array in non-array context → spill or `@`". Legacy Excel canonicalizes "array in scalar context → implicit intersection with caller's row/col". Quantbook v1 Phase 4.7 chooses neither: array-in-scalar-context → `#CALC!` (§ 6.3). This is a defensible safe default; Phase 4.9 lights up `@` syntax + an `xls_calc` mode toggle.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:212:598:- Array operators (`A1:A10 + 1` broadcasting) (Phase 4.10 — needs separate "scalar broadcast across array" semantics).
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:250:8592://! `#NAME?`, `#SPILL!`, `#CALC!`). Four are Quantbook-specific surfaces for kernel/connector
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:295:All rows must have the same number of cells. Mismatched-row literals (e.g. `{1, 2; 3}`) error at parse time with `ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected, found, row }`. **This is a Quantbook v1 subset** — Excel pads with `#N/A` for missing cells, and `{1,2;3}` evaluates as `{1,2;3,#N/A}`. Quantbook v1 chooses loud rejection over silent padding; the pad-with-`#N/A` variant is a Phase 4.10 polish item gated on test-corpus completeness.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:304:- `error literal` — `#NULL!`, `#DIV/0!`, `#VALUE!`, `#REF!`, `#NAME?`, `#NUM!`, `#N/A`, `#SPILL!`, `#CALC!` — the nine Excel-canon error sigils available as `ErrorValue::ALL` entries in `ql-types`. Needed for `FILTER(_, _, #N/A)` etc. (W5-97 closure / Sonnet M2: `#GETTING_DATA` was listed in the original draft but is not in `ErrorValue::ALL` — it's an Excel-Online-specific volatile data-wait state that we don't model in v1; lifting it would require adding the variant alongside `Disconnected`/`Timeout`. Deferred to Phase 4.10 or whenever a real use case appears.)
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:306:NO nested CellRef / RangeRef / Function calls / Unary operators (other than the implicit sign on numeric literals). Codex MEDIUM finding: explicitly document this as a Quantbook v1 subset.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:308:Phase 4.10 lifts to allow refs (`{A1, B1; A2, B2}`) once the array evaluator and dependency extraction prove stable.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.F-codex-review.txt:375:3. Fix by explicitly documenting this as a Quantbook v1 subset and consider allowing error literals like `#N/A`.

PASS today / known Phase 4.10 gap: Treating arrays as no-deps is correct while array cells are literal-only, but once Phase 4.10 allows refs inside array literals, `walk_plan_for_deps` must descend into `ExprPlan::Array` rows instead of treating the array as a leaf. ./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7-design-codex-review.txt:20:This is the Phase 4.7 design doc (Array Formulas + Dynamic Spills) for the Quantbook engine. It is the spec for ~13 sub-phases of implementation work. You are being asked for an independent design review BEFORE any code ships.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7-design-codex-review.txt:114:All rows must have the same number of cells. Mismatched-row literals (e.g. `{1, 2; 3}`) error at parse time with `ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected, found, row }`. Matches Excel canon: Excel pads with `#N/A` but for our v1 we reject loudly — pad-with-N/A is a Phase 4.10 polish item.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7-design-codex-review.txt:118:Array cells in v1: NUMBER / STRING / BOOL / unary-minus-NUMBER only. NO nested CellRef / RangeRef / Function calls inside an array literal. This matches IronCalc's `ArrayNode` model (numbers/strings/booleans/errors). Phase 4.10 can lift to allow `{A1, B1; A2, B2}` if needed.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7-design-codex-review.txt:171:Registration: `register_array_fn(name, sig, f)`. Dispatched by name like the other tiers. Functions that ALWAYS return arrays (SEQUENCE, FILTER, UNIQUE, SORT, TRANSPOSE) register as `ArrayFn`. Functions that CAN return arrays based on input (e.g. an arithmetic op on arrays — Phase 4.7 doesn't do this; that's array-of-scalars broadcasting, Phase 4.10) stay scalar.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7-design-codex-review.txt:458:- Reserved sheet names (`AI`, future): treated as plain SheetName at lex time; binder rejects with `BindError::ReservedSheetName` if needed.

./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7-design-codex-review.txt:1202:- Array constants with cell refs inside: `{A1, B1; A2, B2}` (Phase 4.10).
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7-design-codex-review.txt:1204:- Array operators (`A1:A10 + 1` broadcasting) (Phase 4.10 — needs separate "scalar broadcast across array" semantics).
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7-design-codex-review.txt:1205:- UNIQUE / SORT / SORTBY / RANDARRAY (Phase 4.10).
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7-design-codex-review.txt:1208:- N/A padding for arity-mismatched literals (Phase 4.10 polish).

./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:40:| `Null` | `#NULL!` | Range intersection empty | Reserved; not produced by V1 |
./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:43:| `Spill` | `#SPILL!` | Array-formula spill blocked | Reserved Phase 4.7 |
./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:45:| `Disconnected` | `#DISCONNECTED!` | Network / external data unavailable | Reserved Phase 6.5 connectors |
./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:46:| `Binding` | `#BINDING!` | Late-binding failure | Reserved |
./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:47:| `Timeout` | `#TIMEOUT!` | Operation cancelled / timed out | Reserved Phase 6.1 |
./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:48:| `Permission` | `#PERMISSION!` | Auth / capability denied | Reserved Phase 6 |
./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:84:- 🟡 **divergence** — intentional Quantbook V1 divergence (documented).
./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:85:- ⚪ **not registered / sentinel** — function exists in Excel but not in Quantbook V1's
./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:98:| `COUNT` | Skips errors (canon). **V1 divergence**: skips text/bools UNCONDITIONALLY (Excel canon distinguishes direct literal args from range/ref args — direct `COUNT(TRUE, "1")` counts 2 in Excel but 0 in Quantbook). Provenance-aware dispatch is deferred. | 🟢 errors / 🟡 direct-args | `count_skips_errors_canon`, `count_skips_text_and_bools_v1_divergence`, `count_direct_bool_and_numeric_text_v1_divergence` |
./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:104:### 3.2 Information predicates
./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:117:| `TYPE` | Excel: returns 1/2/4/8/16/64 | ⚪ NOT REGISTERED | — |
./docs/compat/error-matrix.md:119:| `ERROR.TYPE` | Excel: returns 1-7 by error type | ⚪ NOT REGISTERED | — |
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-phase-4.6-design-codex-review.txt:57:It should also define sheet-name validation before rename/add: empty names, duplicate names by canonical form, quote escaping for printer output, and characters invalid for Excel/xlsx (`:`, `\`, `/`, `?`, `*`, `[`, `]`) if Phase 4.11 xlsx import/export compatibility matters. If Quantbook intentionally allows a wider set than Excel, the divergence should be named.
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-phase-4.6-design-codex-review.txt:117:LOW-2: Reserved sheet names are not a binder concern.
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-phase-4.6-design-codex-review.txt:119:The doc says reserved sheet names are treated as plain sheet names at lex time and binder rejects if needed. Reserved/invalid sheet names should be rejected at add/rename/import time, before formulas reference them. Formula parsing should not be the first line of defense for invalid workbook metadata.

./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-68-codex-dates-review.txt:24:   The doc says `DATEVALUE("1900-02-29")` must work [dates-times-formats.md:62](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:62), but it does not decide `DATE(1900,2,29)`, `YEAR(60)`, `MONTH(60)`, `DAY(60)`, or `serial_to_ymd(60)`. IronCalc special-cases parsed text `1900-02-29` to serial 60 [date_and_time.rs:469](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/.references/ironcalc/base/src/functions/date_and_time.rs:469), but its `from_excel_date` maps serials through real `chrono::NaiveDate` [formatter/dates.rs:23](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/.references/ironcalc/base/src/formatter/dates.rs:23), so IronCalc is not a clean oracle for serial 60. Pin Quantbook behavior explicitly.
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-68-codex-dates-review.txt:30:   IronCalc’s date/time function file is 1703 LOC and formatter is 3948 LOC total from the reference read. Even if Quantbook’s scope is smaller, V1 has parsing, permissive `DATE`, `EDATE`/`EOMONTH`, week numbering, business days, and format rendering. The `~500 LOC` date module [dates-times-formats.md:84](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:84) plus 5-day format parser [dates-times-formats.md:225](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md:225) is likely too low.

./docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:67:| SUMIF | ⚠️ | 17 | 4.3 V2 | W5-53 + W5-61: supports number/text/bool/blank criteria + comparators `> < >= <= <> =`. **Wildcards `?` / `*` shipped W5-61** (escape with `~`); apply only to Eq/Ne text criteria, only against text cells (not coerced numbers). 2-arg + 3-arg forms; sum_range default to range. Error in sum_range cell propagates (Excel canon). **W5-60 KNOWN DIVERGENCE**: when `sum_range` size differs from `range`, Quantbook does a flat-index zip; Excel anchors the top-left of `sum_range` and uses `range`'s shape, ignoring `sum_range`'s declared extent. Behavior matches only when `sum_range.len() == range.len()`. Pin Phase 4.3 polish or 4.10. |
./docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:76:| AVERAGEIF | ⚠️ | 6 | 4.3 V2 | W5-55: same predicate suite as SUMIF; #DIV/0! on no matches; optional separate average_range. **W5-60 KNOWN DIVERGENCE**: same as SUMIF — when `average_range` size differs from `range`, Quantbook does a flat-index zip; Excel anchors the top-left of `average_range` and uses `range`'s shape. Behavior matches only when sizes are equal. Pin Phase 4.3 polish or 4.10. |

./docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:123:| CEILING.MATH | ✅ | 7 | 4.3 polish | W5-61: signature `(number, [significance], [mode])`. Significance is `abs(significance)` — sign ignored (unlike CEILING). Default significance=1, mode=0. Positive number always rounds toward +∞; negative number with mode=0 rounds toward +∞ (toward zero), with mode≠0 rounds toward -∞ (away from zero). significance=0 returns 0. CEILING.PRECISE deferred to 4.10. |
./docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:124:| FLOOR.MATH | ✅ | 8 | 4.3 polish | W5-61: signature `(number, [significance], [mode])`. Mirror of CEILING.MATH. Positive number rounds toward -∞; negative+mode=0 toward -∞ (away from zero); negative+mode≠0 toward +∞ (toward zero). significance=0 + nonzero number → #DIV/0! (mirrors FLOOR canon, diverges from CEILING.MATH). FLOOR.PRECISE deferred to 4.10. |

./docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:155:| LEN | ✅ | 3 | 4.3 V1 | **DIVERGES from Excel for emoji ZWJ sequences.** Rust counts Unicode scalar values (`.chars().count()`); Excel counts UTF-16 code units (`LENB`-like for non-BMP). For ASCII / BMP-plane text, both match. ZWJ family emoji like `👨‍👩‍👧`: Quantbook = 5, Excel = 8. (Originally anticipated for Phase 4.9 — that phase shipped R1C1+locale-separators+`@`, NOT UTF-16 string semantics. Awaiting a future Phase.) |
./docs/compat/excel-matrix.md:157:| UPPER | ⚠️ | 2 | 4.3 V1 | Rust's Unicode-default mapping. **DIVERGES from Excel for German ß** (Quantbook = `SS`, Excel = `ß`) and any other locale-sensitive mapping (Turkish I, Greek final sigma). For ASCII-only text both match. (Originally anticipated for Phase 4.9 locale-aware case — that phase did NOT address case mapping. Awaiting a future Phase.) |

./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-63-codex-coercion-review.txt:5:The design is close, but I would not implement from it unchanged. The largest issue is that the doc mixes three different things without clean labels: Excel canon, current Quantbook behavior, and aspirational behavior after later lazy-dispatch work. That causes at least one wrong error-precedence row, under-specifies major existing overrides like `COUNT`/`COUNTA`/information predicates, and risks pinning Quantbook divergences as if they were canonical Excel behavior. The helper migration is directionally right, but the proposed API names should be made less function-aware before promoting them into `ql-types`.
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-63-codex-coercion-review.txt:9:1. **`="x" + #REF!` row is wrong/ambiguous for current Quantbook behavior.**  
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-63-codex-coercion-review.txt:46:C: Override matrix is undercomplete. Add `COUNT`, `COUNTA`, all information predicates, `AVERAGE` empty, `MIN/MAX` empty/all-skipped, `CHOOSE`, `INDEX`, `LARGE/SMALL/MEDIAN/MODE/RANK`, and note unimplemented `TYPE`, `NA`, `ERROR.TYPE`.
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-63-codex-coercion-review.txt:70:3. Split every rule into labels: `Excel canon`, `Current Quantbook`, `Intentional V1 divergence`, or `Aspirational after FN4-03`.
./docs/audits/2026-05-13-w5-63-codex-coercion-review.txt:72:4. Expand §3.4 to include `COUNT`, `COUNTA`, information predicates, `CHOOSE`, `INDEX`, `AVERAGE`, `MIN/MAX`, `LARGE/SMALL`, `MEDIAN`, `MODE`, `RANK/RANK.AVG`, and unimplemented `TYPE`/`NA`/`ERROR.TYPE`.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.G-sonnet-review.md:186:`COUNTA` is in `is_aggregate_function` (plan.rs line 279) and the aggregate expansion fires. `counta()` counts all non-blank values: `c = args.iter().filter(|v| !matches!(v, Value::Blank)).count()`. Three values: `Number(1)`, `Text("")`, `Error(NA)` — none are `Blank` → count = 3. (Note: `""` is a `Text("")`, which is NOT `Blank` in the Quantbook type system. Blank is a distinct variant.)
./docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:304:## 1. Goals (Quantbook UX preferences, not Excel file semantics)
./docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:308:1. **R1C1 reference syntax** as an alternative display + input mode. Toggleable per workbook (a Quantbook UX preference — not stored in Excel files, which are mode-neutral). Storage canon stays A1.
./docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:309:2. **Locale-aware separators** — argument separator, decimal separator, AND array literal separators (Phase 4.7 ships array literals; locale must cover them). Quantbook UX preference; storage stays canonical English.
./docs/audits/2026-05-15-phase-4.9-closing-megaudit-codex.md:472:| 12 | **4.9.L** (W5-148, shipped) | Round-trip + edge case verification suite. +12 end-to-end property tests in `workbook_runtime::tests`: mixed-relativity range rejection at set_formula, `Sales[@Col]` vs `@Sales[Qty]` semantic equivalence, `@A:A` / `@1:1` whole-range narrowing at anchor, `Sheet1!@A1` cross-sheet, R1C1+EN round-trip through op-log replay (set_reference_mode + set_formula both replayed), DE+EN round-trip through replay, `@` preservation through replay, reference_mode/locale changes leave stored text canonical, `@scalar` idempotency, `@SUM(scalar args)` no-op. ./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.6-closing-megaudit-codex.txt:7350:    73	| GAP-F-06 | `COUNT` provenance-aware semantics not implemented. Excel canon: `COUNT(TRUE, "1")` direct args = 2 (counts logical + numeric text); `COUNT(A1:A2)` over the same cells = 0 (skips them as range/ref). Quantbook's eval dispatcher pre-evaluates all args before calling the function body, so `count()` cannot distinguish provenance — always skips text/bool. Filed W5-67 closure (Codex Phase 4.4 mega-audit HIGH 1). Implementing this needs an `ExprPlan`-level marker propagated to the function dispatch (separate from the existing `is_aggregate_function` / RangeAwareFn machinery). | `crates/ql-functions/src/scalar_fns.rs::count` matches only `Value::Number`; pinned by `count_direct_bool_and_numeric_text_v1_divergence` | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 (function library wave 2) or earlier if real-world workbooks surface it |
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.6-closing-megaudit-codex.txt:7351:    74	| GAP-F-08 | `DAYS` text-arg parsing not implemented. Excel canon: `DAYS("2024-12-31", "2024-01-01")` parses each text arg via DATEVALUE first. Quantbook V1 rejects text args with `#VALUE!` (strict-numeric scalar fn). Users should call `DATEVALUE` explicitly. Filed W5-74. | `crates/ql-functions/src/date_fns.rs::days` strict numeric; pinned by `days_text_arg_is_value_error_v1_divergence` | Engine | Engine Phase 4.10 polish |

./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.H-codex-review.txt:147:All rows must have the same number of cells. Mismatched-row literals (e.g. `{1, 2; 3}`) error at parse time with `ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected, found, row }`. **This is a Quantbook v1 subset** — Excel pads with `#N/A` for missing cells, and `{1,2;3}` evaluates as `{1,2;3,#N/A}`. Quantbook v1 chooses loud rejection over silent padding; the pad-with-`#N/A` variant is a Phase 4.10 polish item gated on test-corpus completeness.
./docs/audits/2026-05-14-phase-4.7.H-codex-review.txt:156:- `error literal` — `#NULL!`, `#DIV/0!`, `#VALUE!`, `#REF!`, `#NAME?`, `#NUM!`, `#N/A`, `#SPILL!`, `#CALC!` — the nine Excel-canon error sigils available as `ErrorValue::ALL` entries in `ql-types`. Needed for `FILTER(_, _, #N/A)` etc. (W5-97 closure / Sonnet M2: `#GETTING_DATA` was listed in the original draft but is not in `ErrorValue::ALL` — it's an Excel-Online-specific volatile data-wait state that we don't model in v1; lifting it would require adding the variant alongside `Disconnected`/`Timeout`. Deferred to Phase 4.10 or whenever a real use case appears.)
