     1	# Phase 4.10 — Function Library Wave 2
     2	
     3	**Status:** Design draft, 2026-05-16. Pre-implementation. Codex pre-review pending.
     4	**Spec ID:** FN4-260-01 (≈260-function target), FN4-260-02 (matrix-backed tests), FN4-260-03 (visible errors for unsupported).
     5	**Effort:** 3-5 weeks per MASTER-PLAN § 4.10. This doc scopes the first ~30-50 functions across 8 batches; later waves fill the rest.
     6	
     7	---
     8	
     9	## 0. Context
    10	
    11	The function library shipped 141 functions through Phase 4.5 (W5-58 closed the V1 wave 1 at 100 functions; W5-72 → W5-83 added the date/time/format wave). The compat matrix (`docs/compat/excel-matrix.md`) is **stale** — many functions tagged "Phase 4.5 deferred" actually shipped W5-72 through W5-83. The registry (`crates/ql-functions/src/registry.rs`) is the source of truth.
    12	
    13	Wave 2 fills the remaining gaps toward the v1 260 target. This doc covers 30-50 of them; the rest are split across follow-on waves or explicitly deferred (Engineering, Database, REGEX, byte-variant TEXT).
    14	
    15	## 1. Wave 2 scope (8 batches, ~30-50 functions total)
    16	
    17	| Batch | Subject | Functions | Tier | Est. commits |
    18	|---|---|---|---|---|
    19	| 4.10.A | **Logical fillins** | IFS, IFNA, XOR, SWITCH | ScalarFn | 1 |
    20	| 4.10.B | **Conditional-aggregate fillins** | MINIFS, MAXIFS, COUNTBLANK | RangeAwareFn | 1 |
    21	| 4.10.C | **Stats `*A` variants** | AVERAGEA, MAXA, MINA | RangeAwareFn | 1 |
    22	| 4.10.D | **Info fillins** | NA, ERROR.TYPE, ISFORMULA, TYPE | ScalarFn + ContextAwareFn (ISFORMULA) | 1 |
    23	| 4.10.E | **Math combinatorics + sum variants** | FACT, FACTDOUBLE, COMBIN, COMBINA, PERMUT, PERMUTATIONA, SUMSQ, SUMX2MY2, SUMX2PY2, SUMXMY2 | ScalarFn / RangeAwareFn | 1-2 |
    24	| 4.10.F | **Text utility fillins** | TEXTJOIN, VALUE, CHAR, CODE, UNICODE, UNICHAR, FIXED, DOLLAR | RangeAwareFn (TEXTJOIN) + ScalarFn | 1-2 |
    25	| 4.10.G | **Financial basics** | PMT, FV, PV, NPER, RATE, NPV, IRR | ScalarFn / RangeAwareFn (NPV, IRR) | 2 |
    26	| 4.10.H | **Lookup expansion** | XLOOKUP, XMATCH, OFFSET, INDIRECT, ADDRESS, COLUMN, ROW, COLUMNS, ROWS, FORMULATEXT | RangeAwareFn + ContextAwareFn | 2-3 |
    27	
    28	Total: **~36-49 functions, 10-12 commits.** Each batch follows the W5-51 / W5-54 / W5-58 cadence: one design-batch commit + per-function tests + per-batch matrix update.
    29	
    30	## 2. Out of scope / explicit defer
    31	
    32	- **Engineering family** (BIN2DEC, HEX2BIN, BITAND, etc.) — `post-v1` per existing matrix.
    33	- **Database family** (DGET, DSUM, etc.) — `defer post-v1`.
    34	- **REGEX functions** — Google Sheets extension, not in Excel canon.
    35	- **DBCS byte variants** (LEFTB, RIGHTB, MIDB, LENB, FINDB, SEARCHB) — UTF-16 semantics gap; bundle with the deferred UTF-16 work.
    36	- **TEXTBEFORE / TEXTAFTER / TEXTSPLIT** — array-returning; requires 4.7 array-formula completion for non-trivial cases.
    37	- **AGGREGATE / SUBTOTAL** — depend on 4.7 array-formula completion.
    38	- **NETWORKDAYS.INTL / WORKDAY.INTL** — already deferred per GAP-F-09/10 (holidays + custom weekend mask).
    39	- **CEILING.PRECISE / FLOOR.PRECISE** — already shipped CEILING.MATH/FLOOR.MATH covers most use; precise variants are aliases that need a separate one-off.
    40	
    41	## 3. Batch 1 (4.10.A) — Logical fillins
    42	
    43	### 3.1 Functions
    44	
    45	#### `IFS(test1, value1, test2, value2, ..., [test_default, value_default])`
    46	
    47	Multi-condition selector. Returns `valueK` for the first true `testK`. Excel canon:
    48	- Variadic, even count of args (pairs of test+value).
    49	- No `else` arg: if no test passes, return `#N/A`.
    50	- Errors in tests propagate.
    51	- Empty input → `#VALUE!`.
    52	- Odd arg count → `#N/A` (matches Excel; the trailing unpaired arg is ignored as missing-value rather than syntax error).
    53	
    54	```rust
    55	pub fn ifs(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
    56	    if args.is_empty() { return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value); }
    57	    let mut i = 0;
    58	    while i + 1 < args.len() {
    59	        match &args[i] {
    60	            Value::Error(e) => return Value::Error(*e),
    61	            other => match coercion::to_logical(other) {
    62	                Ok(true) => return args[i + 1].clone(),
    63	                Ok(false) => {}
    64	                Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
    65	            },
    66	        }
    67	        i += 2;
    68	    }
    69	    Value::Error(ErrorValue::Na)
    70	}
    71	```
    72	
    73	#### `IFNA(value, fallback)`
    74	
    75	Like `IFERROR` but only catches `#N/A`. Other errors propagate.
    76	
    77	```rust
    78	pub fn ifna(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
    79	    if args.len() != 2 { return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value); }
    80	    match &args[0] {
    81	        Value::Error(ErrorValue::Na) => args[1].clone(),
    82	        v => v.clone(),
    83	    }
    84	}
    85	```
    86	
    87	#### `XOR(args...)`
    88	
    89	Variadic logical XOR — true iff an odd number of args are true. Mirrors `AND`/`OR` argument handling: skip blanks, coerce non-bool via `to_logical`, propagate errors, require ≥1 non-blank.
    90	
    91	```rust
    92	pub fn xor(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
    93	    if args.is_empty() { return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value); }
    94	    let mut count = 0u64;
    95	    let mut any_non_blank = false;
    96	    for v in args {
    97	        match v {
    98	            Value::Error(e) => return Value::Error(*e),
    99	            Value::Blank => {}
   100	            other => {
   101	                any_non_blank = true;
   102	                match coercion::to_logical(other) {
   103	                    Ok(b) => if b { count += 1 },
   104	                    Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   105	                }
   106	            }
   107	        }
   108	    }
   109	    if !any_non_blank { return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value); }
   110	    Value::Boolean(count % 2 == 1)
   111	}
   112	```
   113	
   114	#### `SWITCH(expression, value1, result1, value2, result2, ..., [default])`
   115	
   116	Matches `expression` against `valueK` (case-insensitive text equality, numeric equality, bool equality). Returns `resultK` on first match. Optional trailing `default` (odd arg count after expression).
   117	
   118	- Variadic ≥3 args (1 expression + at least 1 value+result pair).
   119	- Even count after expression → no default → `#N/A` on no match.
   120	- Odd count after expression → last arg is default.
   121	- Errors in `expression` propagate. Errors in `valueK` skip that pair (NOT propagate — matches Excel SWITCH canon, unlike IFS which propagates test errors).
   122	
   123	```rust
   124	pub fn switch(args: &[Value]) -> Value {
   125	    if args.len() < 3 { return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value); }
   126	    let expr = &args[0];
   127	    if let Value::Error(e) = expr { return Value::Error(*e); }
   128	    let mut i = 1;
   129	    while i + 1 < args.len() {
   130	        let v = &args[i];
   131	        let r = &args[i + 1];
   132	        if !matches!(v, Value::Error(_)) && values_match_for_switch(expr, v) {
   133	            return r.clone();
   134	        }
   135	        i += 2;
   136	    }
   137	    // i == args.len() - 1 → unpaired trailing arg is the default.
   138	    if i < args.len() { return args[i].clone(); }
   139	    Value::Error(ErrorValue::Na)
   140	}
   141	```
   142	
   143	`values_match_for_switch`: case-insensitive String equality, Number/Bool/Blank exact, Error never matches. Open question for Codex pre-review: does Excel allow Number↔String cross-type match (e.g., `SWITCH(1, "1", "match")`)? IronCalc reference says NO (strict type); HyperFormula says YES (coerce). Default to strict.
   144	
   145	### 3.2 Registry additions
   146	
   147	`registry.rs` `default_registry()`:
   148	
   149	```rust
   150	// Phase 4.10.A — logical fillins.
   151	r.register("IFS", scalar_fns::ifs);
   152	r.register("IFNA", scalar_fns::ifna);
   153	r.register("XOR", scalar_fns::xor);
   154	r.register("SWITCH", scalar_fns::switch);
   155	```
   156	
   157	### 3.3 Test plan
   158	
   159	Per function, the existing `scalar_fns::tests` module pattern:
   160	- Happy path (2-3 cases).
   161	- Error propagation (test in IFS, expression in SWITCH).
   162	- Edge cases:
   163	  - IFS: no match → #N/A; odd arg count → #N/A; first match wins.
   164	  - IFNA: #N/A → fallback; other errors → propagate; non-error → value.
   165	  - XOR: 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 trues; all blanks → #VALUE!.
   166	  - SWITCH: no match + no default → #N/A; default branch; first match wins; type-strict equality.
   167	
   168	Target: ≥6 tests per function, ≥24 total. Workspace test count: 2643 → ~2667.
   169	
   170	Plus 1 e2e in `workbook_runtime::tests` exercising each through the full `set_formula` path.
   171	
   172	### 3.4 Acceptance for 4.10.A
   173	
   174	- All 4 functions registered + scalar dispatch.
   175	- Per-function unit tests in `scalar_fns::tests`.
   176	- Matrix entries updated from ❌ to ✅ in `docs/compat/excel-matrix.md` § Logical.
   177	- 7 gates green.
   178	
   179	### 3.5 Excel canon divergences (for matrix Notes)
   180	
   181	- **SWITCH** type-strictness: see § 3.1 open question. Default strict; document in matrix.
   182	- **IFS** odd-arg-count → #N/A (matches Excel; the unpaired arg is silently discarded, not flagged as syntax error).
   183	- **XOR** empty / all-blank → #VALUE! (mirrors AND/OR).
   184	
   185	## 4. Batches 4.10.B-H — brief outlines
   186	
   187	### 4.10.B — Conditional aggregate fillins (MINIFS, MAXIFS, COUNTBLANK)
   188	
   189	Mirror W5-55 SUMIFS/COUNTIFS pattern. All `RangeAwareFn`. COUNTBLANK is simpler (single range, count blanks). MINIFS/MAXIFS share W5-55's criterion-predicate suite + W5-61 wildcards. **W5-60 KNOWN DIVERGENCE** applies (sum_range vs range shape) — surface in matrix Notes.
   190	
   191	### 4.10.C — Stats `*A` variants (AVERAGEA, MAXA, MINA)
   192	
   193	`*A` variants coerce text → 0, bool → 0/1, error propagates. Distinct from base AVERAGE/MAX/MIN which skip text+bool. RangeAwareFn.
   194	
   195	### 4.10.D — Info fillins (NA, ERROR.TYPE, ISFORMULA, TYPE)
   196	
   197	- `NA()` — trivial: returns `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Na)`. Scalar, no args (or 0 args).
   198	- `ERROR.TYPE(value)` — 1=#NULL!, 2=#DIV/0!, 3=#VALUE!, 4=#REF!, 5=#NAME?, 6=#NUM!, 7=#N/A. Non-error → #N/A.
   199	- `TYPE(value)` — 1=number, 2=text, 4=bool, 16=error, 64=array. Scalar.
   200	- `ISFORMULA(reference)` — ContextAwareFn; needs access to `Workbook::formula_at(s,r,c)`. Reference arg semantic is tricky: today's binder passes a Value (the cell's evaluated content), not a reference handle. **Open question**: do we need a parser/binder change to preserve the reference? Probably YES — bundle with a small AST tweak. May defer to a follow-up batch if AST changes balloon scope.
   201	
   202	### 4.10.E — Math combinatorics + sum variants
   203	
   204	- `FACT(n)` — factorial; n<0 → #NUM!; n>170 → #NUM! (overflow).
   205	- `FACTDOUBLE(n)` — double factorial.
   206	- `COMBIN(n,k)`, `COMBINA(n,k)` — combinations (no rep / with rep).
   207	- `PERMUT(n,k)`, `PERMUTATIONA(n,k)` — permutations.
   208	- `SUMSQ`, `SUMX2MY2`, `SUMX2PY2`, `SUMXMY2` — variadic sum-of-squares + cross products. RangeAwareFn for the multi-range pair variants.
   209	
   210	### 4.10.F — Text utility fillins
   211	
   212	- `TEXTJOIN(delim, ignore_empty, args...)` — RangeAwareFn; supersedes CONCAT for the delimiter-controlled case.
   213	- `VALUE(text)` — text → number, locale-aware (decimal separator). ContextAwareFn (uses locale).
   214	- `CHAR(code)`, `CODE(text)`, `UNICODE(text)`, `UNICHAR(code)` — codepoint round-trip.
   215	- `FIXED(number, decimals, no_commas)` — number → text with thousands grouping. Locale-aware.
   216	- `DOLLAR(number, decimals)` — currency text. Locale-aware.
   217	
   218	### 4.10.G — Financial basics
   219	
   220	`PMT / FV / PV / NPER / RATE` — TVM family. Closed-form for PMT/FV/PV/NPER; RATE is iterative (Newton-Raphson with bounded iterations).
   221	
   222	`NPV / IRR` — RangeAwareFn over cash-flow range. IRR is iterative.
   223	
   224	Open question: which Excel sign convention to follow (inflows positive vs outflows positive)? Match Excel canon (outflows negative, signs preserved).
   225	
   226	### 4.10.H — Lookup expansion
   227	
   228	The big one. **XLOOKUP** is the modern replacement for VLOOKUP/HLOOKUP/INDEX+MATCH. Supports 2-way lookup, multiple match modes (exact / next-larger / next-smaller / wildcard / regex-ish), search direction (first/last). RangeAwareFn.
   229	
   230	**XMATCH** — like XLOOKUP but returns position.

    23	/// This test for exact match (modulo case).
    24	///   * strings are not cast into bools or numbers
    25	///   * empty cell is not cast into empty string or zero
    26	pub(crate) fn values_are_equal(left: &CalcResult, right: &CalcResult) -> bool {
    27	    match (left, right) {
    28	        (CalcResult::Number(value1), CalcResult::Number(value2)) => {
    29	            if (value2 - value1).abs() < f64::EPSILON {
    30	                return true;
    31	            }
    32	            false
    33	        }
    34	        (CalcResult::String(value1), CalcResult::String(value2)) => {
    35	            let value1 = value1.to_uppercase();
    36	            let value2 = value2.to_uppercase();
    37	            value1 == value2
    38	        }
    39	        (CalcResult::Boolean(value1), CalcResult::Boolean(value2)) => value1 == value2,
    40	        (CalcResult::EmptyCell, CalcResult::EmptyCell) => true,
    41	        // NOTE: Errors and Ranges are not covered
    42	        (_, _) => false,
    43	    }
    44	}
    45	
    46	// In Excel there are two ways of comparing cell values.
    47	// The old school comparison valid in formulas like D3 < D4 or HLOOKUP,... cast empty cells into empty strings or 0
    48	// For the new formulas like XLOOKUP or SORT an empty cell is always larger than anything else.
    49	
    50	// ..., -2, -1, 0, 1, 2, ..., A-Z, FALSE, TRUE;
    51	pub(crate) fn compare_values(left: &CalcResult, right: &CalcResult) -> i32 {
    52	    match (left, right) {
    53	        (CalcResult::Number(value1), CalcResult::Number(value2)) => {
    54	            let value1 = to_excel_precision(*value1, 15);
    55	            let value2 = to_excel_precision(*value2, 15);
    56	            if (value2 - value1).abs() < f64::EPSILON {
    57	                return 0;
    58	            }
    59	            if value1 < value2 {
    60	                return -1;
    61	            }
    62	            1
    63	        }
    64	        (CalcResult::Number(_value1), CalcResult::String(_value2)) => -1,
    65	        (CalcResult::Number(_value1), CalcResult::Boolean(_value2)) => -1,
    66	        (CalcResult::String(value1), CalcResult::String(value2)) => {
    67	            let value1 = value1.to_uppercase();
    68	            let value2 = value2.to_uppercase();
    69	            match value1.cmp(&value2) {
    70	                std::cmp::Ordering::Less => -1,
    71	                std::cmp::Ordering::Equal => 0,
    72	                std::cmp::Ordering::Greater => 1,
    73	            }
    74	        }
    75	        (CalcResult::String(_value1), CalcResult::Boolean(_value2)) => -1,
    76	        (CalcResult::Boolean(value1), CalcResult::Boolean(value2)) => {
    77	            if value1 == value2 {
    78	                return 0;
    79	            }
    80	            if *value1 {
    81	                return 1;
    82	            }
    83	            -1
    84	        }
    85	        (CalcResult::EmptyCell, CalcResult::String(_value2)) => {
    86	            compare_values(&CalcResult::String("".to_string()), right)
    87	        }
    88	        (CalcResult::String(_value1), CalcResult::EmptyCell) => {
    89	            compare_values(left, &CalcResult::String("".to_string()))
    90	        }
    91	        (CalcResult::EmptyCell, CalcResult::Number(_value2)) => {
    92	            compare_values(&CalcResult::Number(0.0), right)
    93	        }
    94	        (CalcResult::Number(_value1), CalcResult::EmptyCell) => {
    95	            compare_values(left, &CalcResult::Number(0.0))
    96	        }
    97	        (CalcResult::EmptyCell, CalcResult::Boolean(_)) => {
    98	            compare_values(&CalcResult::Boolean(false), right)
    99	        }
   100	        (CalcResult::Boolean(_), CalcResult::EmptyCell) => {
   101	            compare_values(left, &CalcResult::Boolean(false))
   102	        }
   103	        (CalcResult::EmptyCell, CalcResult::EmptyCell) => 0,
   104	        // Errors sort after everything else, ordered by Excel's canonical rank
   105	        (CalcResult::Error { error: e1, .. }, CalcResult::Error { error: e2, .. }) => {
   106	            let r1 = error_sort_rank(e1);
   107	            let r2 = error_sort_rank(e2);
   108	            if r1 < r2 {
   109	                -1
   110	            } else if r1 > r2 {
   111	                1
   112	            } else {
   113	                0
   114	            }
   115	        }
   116	        (CalcResult::Error { .. }, _) => 1,
   117	        (_, CalcResult::Error { .. }) => -1,
   118	        (_, _) => 1,
   119	    }
   120	}

    80	/// `values` is a `Vec<Value>` rather than a slice because the
    81	/// producer (eval) owns the buffer. For very large ranges (whole-
    82	/// column refs over millions of cells) this is the same cost as the
    83	/// existing flattening path; range-aware functions get both the
    84	/// flat data and the structure.
    85	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
    86	pub enum FnArg {
    87	    Scalar(Value),
    88	    Range {
    89	        values: Vec<Value>,
    90	        rows: usize,
    91	        cols: usize,
    92	    },
    93	}
    94	
    95	impl FnArg {
    96	    /// Construct a 1D `Range` (1 row × N cols, or N rows × 1 col —
    97	    /// caller decides which axis). Convenience for tests + 1D
    98	    /// consumers; uses `rows = 1, cols = values.len()` by default.
    99	    /// Production callers should construct the variant directly so
   100	    /// the shape reflects the source range.
   101	    #[cfg(test)]
   102	    pub fn range_1d(values: Vec<Value>) -> Self {
   103	        let cols = values.len();
   104	        FnArg::Range {
   105	            values,
   106	            rows: 1,
   107	            cols,
   108	        }
   109	    }
   110	
   111	    /// Construct a 2D `Range` with explicit shape.
   112	    #[cfg(test)]
   113	    pub fn range_2d(values: Vec<Value>, rows: usize, cols: usize) -> Self {
   114	        debug_assert_eq!(rows * cols, values.len(), "shape mismatch");
   115	        FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols }
   116	    }
   117	
   118	    /// Convenience for tests + scalar-only call sites: returns the
   119	    /// inner `Value` if this is a scalar arg, panics otherwise.
   120	    /// Production code should `match` on the variant explicitly.
   121	    #[cfg(test)]
   122	    pub fn unwrap_scalar(&self) -> &Value {
   123	        match self {
   124	            FnArg::Scalar(v) => v,
   125	            FnArg::Range { .. } => panic!("FnArg::unwrap_scalar called on a Range arg"),
   126	        }
   127	    }
   128	
   129	    /// Convenience for tests: returns the inner values if Range,
   130	    /// panics otherwise.
   131	    #[cfg(test)]
   132	    pub fn unwrap_range(&self) -> &[Value] {
   133	        match self {
   134	            FnArg::Range { values, .. } => values.as_slice(),
   135	            FnArg::Scalar(_) => panic!("FnArg::unwrap_range called on a Scalar arg"),
   136	        }
   137	    }
   138	}

   140	/// Signature for range-aware built-in functions. The evaluator
   141	/// constructs the arg list with the correct `FnArg` variant per
   142	/// position before dispatching.
   143	pub type RangeAwareFn = fn(&[FnArg]) -> Value;

     1	use crate::expressions::token::Error;
     2	
     3	// Here we use some numerical routines to solve for some functions:
     4	// RATE, IRR, XIRR
     5	// We use a combination of heuristics, bisection and Newton-Raphson
     6	// If you want to improve on this methods you probably would want to find failing tests first
     7	
     8	// From Microsoft docs:
     9	// https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/office/rate-function-9f665657-4a7e-4bb7-a030-83fc59e748ce
    10	// Returns the interest rate per period of an annuity.
    11	// RATE is calculated by iteration (using Newton-Raphson) and can have zero or more solutions.
    12	// If the successive results of RATE do not converge to within 0.0000001 after 20 iterations,
    13	// RATE returns the #NUM! error value.
    14	// NOTE: We need a better algorithm here
    15	pub(crate) fn compute_rate(
    16	    pv: f64,
    17	    fv: f64,
    18	    nper: f64,
    19	    pmt: f64,
    20	    annuity_type: i32,
    21	    guess: f64,
    22	) -> Result<f64, (Error, String)> {
    23	    let mut rate = guess;
    24	    // Excel _claims_ to do 20 iterations, but that will have tests failing
    25	    let max_iterations = 50;
    26	    let eps = 0.0000001;
    27	    let annuity_type = annuity_type as f64;
    28	    if guess <= -1.0 {
    29	        return Err((Error::VALUE, "Rate initial guess must be > -1".to_string()));
    30	    }
    31	    for _ in 1..=max_iterations {
    32	        let t = (1.0 + rate).powf(nper - 1.0);
    33	        let tt = t * (1.0 + rate);
    34	        let f = pv * tt + pmt * (1.0 + rate * annuity_type) * (tt - 1.0) / rate + fv;
    35	        let f_prime = pv * nper * t - pmt * (tt - 1.0) / (rate * rate)
    36	            + pmt * (1.0 + rate * annuity_type) * t * nper / rate;
    37	        let new_rate = rate - f / f_prime;
    38	        if new_rate <= -1.0 {
    39	            return Err((Error::NUM, "Failed to converge".to_string()));
    40	        }
    41	        if (new_rate - rate).abs() < eps {
    42	            return Ok(new_rate);
    43	        }
    44	        rate = new_rate;
    45	    }
    46	    Err((Error::NUM, "Failed to converge".to_string()))
    47	}
    48	
    49	pub(crate) fn compute_npv(rate: f64, values: &[f64]) -> Result<f64, (Error, String)> {
    50	    let mut npv = 0.0;
    51	    for (i, item) in values.iter().enumerate() {
    52	        npv += item / (1.0 + rate).powi(i as i32 + 1)
    53	    }
    54	    Ok(npv)
    55	}
    56	
    57	// Tries to solve npv(r, values) = 0 for r given values
    58	// Uses a bit of heuristics:
    59	// * First tries Newton-Raphson around the guess
    60	// * Failing that uses bisection and bracketing
    61	// * If that fails (no root found of the interval) uses Newton-Raphson around the edges
    62	// Values for x1, x2 and the guess for N-R are fine tuned using heuristics
    63	pub(crate) fn compute_irr(values: &[f64], guess: f64) -> Result<f64, (Error, String)> {
    64	    if guess <= -1.0 {
    65	        return Err((Error::VALUE, "Rate initial guess must be > -1".to_string()));
    66	    }
    67	    // The values cannot be all positive or all negative
    68	    if values.iter().all(|&x| x >= 0.0) || values.iter().all(|&x| x <= 0.0) {
    69	        return Err((Error::NUM, "Failed to converge".to_string()));
    70	    }

   149	pub(crate) fn is_volatile_function(name: &str) -> bool {
   150	    matches!(
   151	        name,
   152	        // Time / date — value changes every recompute.
   153	        "NOW" | "TODAY"
   154	        // Pseudo-random — value changes every recompute.
   155	        | "RAND" | "RANDBETWEEN" | "RANDARRAY"
   156	        // Address-by-string — result depends on workbook structure;
   157	        // a cell rename anywhere can affect the result.
   158	        | "INDIRECT"
   159	        // Structural offset — result depends on the current grid.
   160	        | "OFFSET"
   161	        // Environment introspection.
   162	        | "INFO" | "CELL"
   163	    )
   164	}
   165	
   166	/// Phase 3.2 — direct-dep collection extracted from walking a bound
   167	/// `ExprPlan`. Fields are owned (no borrows) so the caller can pass
   168	/// the collection around or store it without lifetime ceremony.
   169	///
   170	/// `cells`, `named_ranges`, and `names` are deduplicated by the
   171	/// `extract_and_register_deps` orchestrator (the walker emits
   172	/// duplicates; the orchestrator collapses).
   173	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
   174	pub struct FormulaDeps {
   175	    /// Direct cell references (`ExprPlan::CellRef`). Resolved sheet
   176	    /// is included.
   177	    pub cells: Vec<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)>,
   178	    /// Named-range references (`ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef`). Each
   179	    /// entry pairs the canonical (uppercase) name with the resolved
   180	    /// `Range` payload.
   181	    pub named_ranges: Vec<(Arc<str>, Range)>,
   182	    /// Names referenced by the formula. Currently only names that
   183	    /// resolve to ranges populate this (via `AggregateNameRef`); see
   184	    /// module docs for the limitation around scalar-resolved names.
   185	    pub names: Vec<Arc<str>>,
   186	    /// **W5-154 (Phase 4.8.G.3 foundation):** table names referenced by
   187	    /// the formula (via `ExprPlan::StructuredRef.table_name`). Used by
   188	    /// the `table_to_formulas` reverse index for `on_table_*`
   189	    /// mutation hooks. Populated alongside `named_ranges` (the
   190	    /// resolved range still goes there for stripe registration).
   191	    pub tables: Vec<Arc<str>>,
   192	    /// `true` iff any volatile function appears anywhere in the
   193	    /// plan tree.
   194	    pub is_volatile: bool,
   195	}
   196	
   197	impl FormulaDeps {
   198	    /// Total dependency count (cells + named ranges). Useful for
   199	    /// summary metrics; Phase 3.10 megaudit / ql-profile will surface
   200	    /// per-formula dep counts in the graph-profile JSON.
   201	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
   202	        self.cells.len() + self.named_ranges.len()
   203	    }
   204	
   205	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   206	        self.cells.is_empty() && self.named_ranges.is_empty()
   207	    }
   208	}
   209	
   210	/// Phase 3.2 — recursive walker. Accumulates dependencies + volatile-
   211	/// function presence by descending the `ExprPlan` tree. Does not
   212	/// allocate any nodes or touch any graph state; pure read.
   213	pub(crate) fn walk_plan_for_deps(plan: &ExprPlan, deps: &mut FormulaDeps) {
   214	    match plan {
   215	        ExprPlan::Number(_) | ExprPlan::Bool(_) | ExprPlan::String(_) => {
   216	            // Leaf literals carry no dependencies.
   217	        }
   218	        ExprPlan::CellRef {
   219	            sheet, row, col, ..
   220	        } => {
   221	            deps.cells.push((*sheet, *row, *col));
   222	        }
   223	        ExprPlan::Binary { lhs, rhs, .. } => {
   224	            walk_plan_for_deps(lhs, deps);
   225	            walk_plan_for_deps(rhs, deps);
   226	        }
   227	        ExprPlan::Unary { operand, .. } => {
   228	            walk_plan_for_deps(operand, deps);
   229	        }
   230	        ExprPlan::Function { name, args } => {
   231	            if is_volatile_function(name) {
   232	                deps.is_volatile = true;
   233	            }
   234	            for arg in args {
   235	                walk_plan_for_deps(arg, deps);
   236	            }
   237	        }
   238	        ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { name, range } => {
   239	            deps.named_ranges.push((Arc::clone(name), *range));
   240	            deps.names.push(Arc::clone(name));
   241	        }
   242	        // **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** array-literal and error-literal plans
   243	        // have NO dependencies — they're pure constants. Array cells
   244	        // are also restricted to literals (Number/Bool/String/Error),
   245	        // so even walking the inner cells would yield zero deps.

codex
**HIGH**

- `SWITCH` design should change before implementation. Excel appears type-strict for number-vs-text matching: `SWITCH(1,"1","Text",1,"Number")` returns the numeric match, not the text one. IronCalc is also strict: `compare_values` ranks Number vs String as non-equal in [.references/ironcalc/base/src/functions/util.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/.references/ironcalc/base/src/functions/util.rs:51). Also fix the doc’s error rule: IronCalc propagates errored case values, it does not skip them, in [.references/ironcalc/base/src/functions/logical/switch.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/.references/ironcalc/base/src/functions/logical/switch.rs:24). Microsoft’s SWITCH page confirms exact value matching and no-match `#N/A`; a concrete Excel example for `1` vs `"1"` is here: https://www.getelyxai.com/en/excel-formulas/logical/switch.

- Reference-tier work should be broken out as its own design doc, not hidden inside 4.10.D. Current `FnArg` carries only `Scalar` or flattened `Range`, with no address/provenance handle in [range_aware_fns.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-functions/src/range_aware_fns.rs:86). Smallest footprint: ship scalar info funcs first; create a small “ReferenceArg/ReferenceAwareFn” mini-phase for `ISFORMULA`, `FORMULATEXT`, `ROW/COLUMN`, `ROWS/COLUMNS`, and maybe `ADDRESS`.

- Defer `OFFSET` and `INDIRECT` from v1 Wave 2. Marking them volatile is not enough: dep extraction walks bind-time args only and cannot discover runtime target ranges in [calcgraph_session.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs:230). A compile-time-resolvable subset is not worth the misleading support surface.

**MEDIUM**

- Financial scope is undercut. The matrix says TVM includes `IPMT`/`PPMT`, and cash-flow includes `XNPV`/`XIRR`/`MIRR`; the doc only includes `PMT/FV/PV/NPER/RATE/NPV/IRR`. Either explicitly defer the missing ones or add `IPMT`/`PPMT` to 4.10.G. IronCalc’s sign convention is standard Excel TVM: `pv*(1+rate)^nper + pmt*(1+rate*type)*... + fv = 0`; `type=0` end, `type=1` beginning, with nonzero type treated as begin in [.references/ironcalc/base/src/functions/financial.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/.references/ironcalc/base/src/functions/financial.rs:176).

- Scope completeness misses Information scalars from the matrix: `ISEVEN`, `ISODD`, `ISREF`, `ISNONTEXT`, and `N`. Add the easy scalar ones to 4.10.D; defer `ISREF` with reference-tier work.

- Matrix-tagged `4.10` functions not covered: percentile/quartile, correlation/regression, distributions, `SUBTOTAL`, `LOOKUP`, depreciation, engineering/base conversion, `ERF/ERFC`. The doc can keep them out of Wave 2, but it should say Wave 2 is not “closes v1 260” unless follow-on waves are explicitly listed.

- Batch order: move low-risk scalar/range batches before text/financial/reference. Suggested: A logical, B conditional aggregates, C `*A` + info scalars, D math, E text, F financial, G `XMATCH/XLOOKUP/ADDRESS`, H reference-tier design/implementation subset. Keep `OFFSET/INDIRECT` out.

- Budget: 10-12 commits across 2-3 sessions is optimistic if `XLOOKUP`, financial iteration, locale text formatting, and reference-tier are all included. Without `OFFSET/INDIRECT` and with reference-tier split out, 12-16 commits across 3-4 sessions is more realistic.

**LOW**

- Audit after D/E is fine if reference-tier is split out. If not, audit immediately after the first reference-tier patch and again before `XLOOKUP`.

- Out-of-scope mostly looks right. `SUBTOTAL` is debatable: it is widely used, but without hidden-row/filter semantics it will be a partial implementation. Keep deferred and document why.

- Reserved/Quantbook-specific: `CA`, `ECHO`, `FOO` are test fixtures only, not matrix functions. I found no real `SUM.LARGE`-style reserved functions in the current matrix.

- Widely used Excel functions not in the matrix at all: `LET`, `TAKE`, `DROP`, `VSTACK`, `HSTACK`, `TOCOL`, `TOROW`, `WRAPROWS`, `WRAPCOLS`, `EXPAND`, `FORMULATEXT`. Add rows now, even if most are deferred.
